<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that strict comparison in the extended-functional sense implies Jiang–Su absorption for separable simple nuclear non-elementary <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebras. This resolves the corresponding implication of the Toms–Winter regularity problem, including nonunital algebras and allowing unbounded traces. More generally, every separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra whose Cuntz semigroup is almost unperforated and fully almost divisible absorbs the Jiang–Su algebra.

- **claim-simple-comparison-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-thm-main)): Let $A$ be a separable simple nuclear non-elementary $C^*$-algebra. If $A$ has strict comparison in the sense of Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>, then $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: Extended-functional strict comparison implies Jiang–Su absorption under separability, simplicity, nuclearity and non-elementarity, including the nonunital setting.
- **claim-condition-c-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-condition-c)): Let $A$ be a nonzero separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>. For every fixed free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there is a unital $*$-homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. Consequently $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: The Cuntz-semigroup order conditions yield a unital central Jiang–Su homomorphism and tensorial absorption without simplicity.
- **claim-canonical-cu-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-canonical)): Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra, and let $\iota:A\to A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$ be $\iota(a)=a\otimes1_\mathcal Z$. If $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota):\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z)$ is an isomorphism, then $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: An isomorphism induced by the canonical first-factor embedding suffices for Jiang–Su absorption.
- **claim-abstract-cu-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-abstract-cu)): Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra. Then $$A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \mathrm{Cu}(A)\cong\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z) \text{ in the category }\mathbf{Cu}.$$ The isomorphism on the right is abstract: it is not required to be induced by the first-factor embedding, or by any $*$-homomorphism.
  - Explanation: An abstract isomorphism of completed Cuntz semigroups characterizes Jiang–Su absorption for separable nuclear algebras; it need not arise from an algebra homomorphism.
- **claim-cut-placement-witness-bounds** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement)): Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.* 1. *If $a,b\in D_+$ and $\\|a-r^*br\\|<\epsilon$, then there is $v\in D$ with $$v^*v=(a-\epsilon)_+,\qquad vv^*\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b).$$ Consequently, if $a\precsim b$, the hereditary algebra of every positive cut of $a$ can be placed in $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b)$ by a polar isometry.* 2. *If $a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}(b)_+$, then for every $\epsilon>0$ there is a finite integer $L$ such that $$[(a-\epsilon)_+]\le L[b].$$ If $[a]\le L[b]$, every positive cut of $a$ still compares to $L[(b-\delta)_+]$ for a sufficiently small $\delta>0$.* 3. *Comparisons between elements of a hereditary subalgebra can be witnessed in that subalgebra. Comparisons between specified matrix corners can be witnessed by the appropriate rectangular matrices.* 4. *If $u\precsim(y-\beta)_+$, where $0\le\alpha<\beta$, then for each $\eta>0$ there is $r\in D$ such that $$\\|u-r^*(y-\alpha)_+r\\|<\eta,\qquad \\|r\\|^2\le\frac{\\|u\\|+\eta}{\beta-\alpha}.$$
  - Explanation: Positive cuts admit hereditary placement, ideal membership gives finite cut comparisons, and a spectral margin bounds comparison witnesses.
- **claim-comparison-almost-unperforation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-convention)): For any $C^*$-algebra $A$, strict comparison in the sense of Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> is equivalent to almost unperforation of $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$.
  - Explanation: Extended-functional strict comparison and almost unperforation are equivalent, with ideal containment retained through the non-strict inequalities.
- **claim-small-nonzero-classes** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-lem-small)): If $A$ is simple and non-elementary, then $B=A\otimes\mathcal K$ is simple and has no minimal projections. For every nonzero $e\in B_+$ and integer $M\ge1$, there is a nonzero $h\in B_+$ with $$M[h]\le[e].$$
  - Explanation: Simplicity and non-elementarity provide arbitrarily small nonzero Cuntz classes below a prescribed nonzero positive element.
- **claim-functional-trichotomy** (classification, [source](#stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy)): Let $A$ be simple and nonzero. Every functional on $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ is either zero, infinite on every nonzero class, or positive on every nonzero class and finite on every class $[(c-\delta)_+]$ with $c\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+$ and $\delta>0$. The third type consists exactly of the functionals finite and positive at some class.
  - Explanation: Functionals are zero, everywhere infinite on nonzero classes, or positive on nonzero classes and finite on positive spectral cuts; finiteness on all classes is not asserted.
- **claim-trace-transfer-small-support** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-lem-transfer)): Let $B$ be a simple stable $C^*$-algebra, let $a\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $0\ne h\in B_+$. Suppose that $r:[0,1]\to[0,\infty)$ is continuous and vanishes on $[0,\delta]$ for some $0<\delta<1$. There is $c\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_B(h)_+$ such that $$\tau(c)=\tau(r(a))$$ for every lower-semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $B$.
  - Explanation: A continuous function vanishing near zero can have its trace values transferred into any prescribed nonzero hereditary support, simultaneously for all extended traces.
- **claim-integer-spectral-rounding** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-lem-rounding)): Let $B$ be a stable $C^*$-algebra, $c\in B_+$, and let $L$ be an integer greater than $\\|c\\|$. Put $$w=\sum_{i=1}^L[(c-i)_+]\in\mathrm{Cu}(B).$$ For every lower-semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$, writing $\varphi=d_\tau$, one has $$\label{ext:eq:rounding} \varphi(w)\le\tau(c)\le\varphi(w)+d_\tau(c).$$
  - Explanation: Integer spectral cuts approximate the trace from below with error bounded by the support rank, independently of the number of spectral levels.
- **claim-full-almost-divisibility** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-thm-divisibility)): Let $A$ be a simple exact non-elementary $C^*$-algebra with strict comparison as in Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>. For every $x\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and integer $N\ge1$, there exists $u\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ such that $Nu\le x\le(N+1)u$.
  - Explanation: Summable relative rounding errors and strict comparison produce a single dividing class satisfying both order inequalities. Separability and nuclearity are unnecessary for this result.
- **claim-condition-c-from-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ext-cor-condition-c)): Let $A$ be a simple exact non-elementary $C^*$-algebra with strict comparison in the sense of Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>. Then $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ satisfies condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>.
  - Explanation: The simple exact comparison hypothesis supplies both almost unperforation and full almost divisibility.
- **claim-unital-uniform-rank-approximation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-unital-approx)): Let $A$ be a unital simple infinite-dimensional $C^*$-algebra with nonempty tracial state space $T(A)$. For every $p\in A_+$ and $\eta>0$, there are a finite integer $L$ and $w\in M_L(A)_+$ such that $$0\le\tau(p)-d_\tau(w)\le\eta \qquad(\tau\in T(A)),$$ where matrix traces are unnormalized. No exactness, nuclearity, or comparison assumption is required.
  - Explanation: Small hereditary support and integer rounding approximate positive-element trace values uniformly from below over tracial states, without exactness, nuclearity or comparison.
- **claim-canonical-map-transfers-condition-c** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-c)): Under the hypothesis of Corollary <a href="#cor:canonical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:canonical">3</a>, the semigroup $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ satisfies condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>, namely Equations <a href="#eq:pre:au" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:au">[eq:pre:au]</a>–<a href="#eq:pre:div" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:div">[eq:pre:div]</a>.
  - Explanation: The canonical Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism transports almost unperforation and tensorially constructed divisibility back to the original algebra.
- **claim-uniform-rank-gap-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-g)): Suppose $A$ is exact and satisfies <a href="#eq:pre:au" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:au">[eq:pre:au]</a>. If $v,w\in A_+$ satisfy $$d_\tau(v)\le\tfrac12d_\tau(w)$$ for every lower semicontinuous extended trace on $A$, then $v\precsim w$.
  - Explanation: Exactness identifies functionals with extended traces, and the uniform rank gap permits strict comparison even at zero and infinite trace values.
- **claim-divisible-cone-placement** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-placement)): Assume <a href="#eq:pre:au" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:au">[eq:pre:au]</a>–<a href="#eq:pre:div" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:div">[eq:pre:div]</a>.* 1. *Let $B$ be a hereditary subalgebra of a matrix algebra over $A\otimes\mathcal K$, let $b\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $N\ge1$ and $\epsilon>0$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\psi:M_N\to\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$ such that $$\\|\sigma(\psi(1))\\|=1 \quad\text{whenever}\quad \sigma((b-\epsilon)_+)\ne0.$$ Its $N$ diagonal weights are orthogonal and equivalent in $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$. Each diagonal weight, cut at $1/2$, dominates a positive class $u_0$ for which $$[(b-\epsilon)_+]\le(N+1)u_0.$$* 2. *Let $C$ be isomorphic to a finite direct sum of hereditary subalgebras as above. For $b\in C_+$ a contraction, $k\ge1$, and $0<q\le1$, there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\psi:M_k\to\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_C(b)$, with weight $t=\psi(1)$, such that $$\label{eq:pre:P} d_\sigma(t)\le q\,d_\sigma(b),\qquad d_\sigma((t-\tfrac12)_+) \ge(q/3)d_\sigma((b-\tfrac12)_+)$$ for every bounded positive trace $\sigma$ on $C$.
  - Explanation: Divisible classes and hereditary cut placement yield equivalent matrix-cone corners with either representation visibility or bounded-trace rank control.
- **claim-positive-pencil-support-compression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-support)): Let $K=K^*$ be a bounded operator on a Hilbert space, let $s\ge0$ be bounded, and let $\lambda\in\mathbb R$. On the support of $$b=s[s(\lambda-K)s]_+s$$ the compression of $K$ is at most $\lambda$. If $b=0$, then $s(\lambda-K)s\le0$.
  - Explanation: A positive-part pencil restricts the compression of the error operator on its resulting support without a commutativity assumption; a zero output gives the complementary operator inequality.
- **claim-bounded-ultrapower-diagonalization** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-diagonal)): Suppose a countable family of norm-continuous algebraic and functional-calculus conditions, with fixed coefficients in a norm ultrapower, is approximately satisfiable on every finite subfamily. Suppose each unknown has a fixed individual norm bound in these finite solutions. Then the conditions can be satisfied exactly in the ultrapower. Additional countably many commutator conditions and conditions after multiplication by a fixed countable subset of the algebra may be imposed in the same way.
  - Explanation: Approximate finite satisfiability becomes exact countable satisfiability in the norm ultrapower when every unknown retains its individual norm bound.
- **claim-two-support-shrinking** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-shrinking)): There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>. If $a,b\in A$ are positive contractions and $z\in\widetilde A$ satisfies $\\|p_a z p_b\\|\le1$, there are positive contractions $a'\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(a)$ and $b'\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b)$ such that $$\label{shr:conclusion} \\|p_{a'}z p_{b'}\\|\le c$$ and, for every irreducible representation $\sigma$ of $A$, $$\label{shr:visibility} \max\{\\|\sigma(a)\\|,\\|\sigma(b)\\|\}\ge\tfrac12 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \sigma(a')\ne0\ \text{or}\ \sigma(b')\ne0.$$ The support norms are computed in the bidual, or equivalently in the universal representation.
  - Explanation: A universal contraction factor reduces cross-compression while preserving nonvanishing in each triggered irreducible representation. The proof establishes existence rather than an explicit value of the factor.
- **claim-two-support-transfer-contraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer)): Suppose that $A$ satisfies <span class="upright">(C)</span>. Let $B\subseteq A$ be hereditary, let $D\in M_l(B)_+$ and $E\in B_+$, and suppose that $w_1,\ldots,w_m\in M_{l,1}(B)$ satisfy $$w_iw_i^*\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(D),\qquad w_i^*w_i\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(E).$$ For each $\delta>0$ there is a contraction $v\in M_{l,1}(B)$ such that $$vv^*\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(D),\qquad v^*v\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(E),$$ and, in every representation $\sigma$ of $B$, $$\max_{1\le i\le m}\\|\sigma(w_i)\\|\ge\delta \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \\|\sigma(v)\\|=1.$$
  - Explanation: Divisibility and cut placement replace finitely many rectangular support elements by a contraction visible with norm one wherever a prescribed element exceeds the threshold.
- **claim-coordinate-norm-visible-central-cones** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-norm)): Suppose that $A$ is separable and satisfies <span class="upright">(C)</span>. For every positive contraction $x\in A$, every $\gamma>0$, and every integer $k\ge2$, there are c.p.c. maps $\nu_n:M_k\to A$ such that $$\nu:M_k\longrightarrow A_\omega\cap A', \qquad \nu(z)=[(\nu_n(z))_n],$$ is order zero, and there is a number $\kappa>0$ for which $$\label{norm:coordinate-visibility} \begin{gathered} \text{for every }n\in\mathbb N\text{ and every irreducible representation }\sigma \text{ of }A,\\ \\|\sigma(x)\\|\ge\gamma \ \Longrightarrow\ \\|\sigma(x\nu_n(1))\\|\ge\kappa . \end{gathered}$$ One may take $\kappa=\gamma^2/32$. In particular, the estimate holds on one $\omega$-large set of coordinates simultaneously for every irreducible representation, including representations chosen separately at each coordinate.
  - Explanation: Norm-central order-zero cones detect the prescribed positive contraction at coordinates simultaneously over all irreducible representations, including representations varying with the coordinate.
- **claim-finite-representation-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-norm-finite-cover)): Let $x\in A_+$, $\gamma>0$, and let $(s_\alpha)_{\alpha\in\Lambda}$ be positive elements of $A$. Suppose that $\mathcal K(x,\gamma)\ne\varnothing$ and that, for every $\sigma\in\mathcal K(x,\gamma)$, some $\sigma(s_\alpha)$ is nonzero. Then there are $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_m$ and $t>0$ such that $$\sigma\in\mathcal K(x,\gamma) \quad\Longrightarrow\quad \max_{1\le i\le m}\\|\sigma(s_{\alpha_i})\\|>t.$$ Thus the nonvanishing loci of the cuts $(s_{\alpha_i}-t)_+$ still cover $\mathcal K(x,\gamma)$. For a finite covering family of positive contractions, continuous functional calculus can replace its members by positive contractions with the same supports and with maximum represented norm equal to one at every $\sigma\in\mathcal K(x,\gamma)$.
  - Explanation: Ideal quotient norms yield a finite cut cover of the representation locus, without compactness of the primitive ideal space.
- **claim-pure-state-compression** (construction, [source](#stmt-norm-excision)): Let $\lambda$ be a pure state of a unital $C^*$-algebra $D$, with irreducible GNS representation $(\pi,\mathcal H,\xi)$. Given a finite subset $\mathcal E\subset D$ and $\epsilon>0$, there is a positive contraction $q\in D$ such that $$\pi(q)\xi=\xi,\qquad \\|qyq-\lambda(y)q^2\\|<\epsilon\quad(y\in\mathcal E).$$
  - Explanation: Pure-state excision compresses a finite family to its scalar state values while fixing the cyclic vector.
- **claim-finite-word-prototype** (construction, [source](#stmt-norm-prototype)): For this finite word model there is a positive contractive seed $s\in A$ with $\sigma(s)\ne0$, whose support $P=p_s$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} PxP&\ge(\gamma/4)P, \label{norm:positive-compression}\\ \big\\|(Pb_i^*b_jP)_{i,j=1}^m-I_m\otimes P\big\\| &\le\eta^2/4, \label{norm:local-gram}\\ \big\\|(Pr_i(a)^*r_j(a)P)_{l_i,l_j<d}\big\\| &\le\eta^2/4\qquad(a\in F). \label{norm:local-residual} \end{aligned}$$ Every positive element in $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(s)$ has a support on which the same three estimates hold.
  - Explanation: Excision supplies a nonzero hereditary seed controlling positive compression, the word Gram matrix and residual intertwining errors; further hereditary restriction preserves these estimates.
- **claim-separated-representation-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-norm-separated-cover)): Assume that $A$ satisfies <span class="upright">(C)</span>. Suppose that positive contractions $s_1,\ldots,s_m\in A$ have nonvanishing loci covering $\mathcal K(x,\gamma)$. For each pair $i\ne j$, prescribe a finite set $\mathcal E_{ij}\subset\widetilde A$, and let $\delta>0$. There are positive contractions $t_i\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(s_i)$, with supports $Q_i=p_{t_i}$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \max_i\\|\sigma(t_i)\\|&=1 &&(\sigma\in\mathcal K(x,\gamma)), \label{norm:retained-cover}\\ \\|Q_i z Q_j\\|&\le\delta &&(i\ne j,\ z\in\mathcal E_{ij}). \label{norm:cross-compressions} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Iterated support shrinking retains a finite representation cover while reducing all prescribed cross-compressions; later restrictions preserve earlier bounds.
- **claim-finite-stage-central-cones** (bound, [source](#stmt-norm-finite-stage)): Assume that $A$ satisfies <span class="upright">(C)</span>. Let $x\in A$ be a positive contraction, $\gamma>0$, $k\ge2$, $F\subset A$ a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, $d\ge1$, and $0<\eta<1$. There is a c.p.c. map $V:M_k\to A$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|V(z)V(w)\\|&\le\eta^2 &&(z,w\in(M_k)_+,\ \\|z\\|,\\|w\\|\le1,\ zw=0), \label{norm:order-zero-error}\\ \\|[a,V(z)]\\|&\le2\eta+2/d &&(a\in F,\ \\|z\\|\le1), \label{norm:central-error}\\ \\|\sigma(xV(1))\\|&\ge\gamma^2/32 &&(\sigma\in\mathcal K(x,\gamma)). \label{norm:finite-visibility} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: The compressed row and level taper give simultaneous order-zero, commutator and representation-visibility estimates for a finite test set.
- **claim-bounded-trace-central-cones** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bounded-trace)): Fix $k\geq2$ and put $$L=(20k)^2,\qquad C_k=1+4L.$$ Let $F\subset D$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, let $\mathcal P\subseteq F$ consist of positive contractions, and let $0<\delta<1$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $V:M_k\to D$ such that, for every $\tau\in T_{\leq1}(D)$, $$\begin{aligned} \sup_{\\|x\\|\leq1}\\|[a,V(x)]\\|_{2,\tau} &<\varepsilon &&(a\in F),\label{tr:central-target}\\ \tau(aV(1)) &\geq \frac\delta3\tau(a)-C_k\delta^{3/2}-\varepsilon &&(a\in\mathcal P).\label{tr:mass-target} \end{aligned}$$ In particular, $k$ is fixed before the accuracy is chosen; no bound on the number of nuclear approximation summands is required.
  - Explanation: Convex nuclear approximations and support compressions produce exact order-zero cones with tracial centrality and controlled mass loss, independently of the number of approximation summands.
- **claim-tracial-lipschitz-calculus** (bound, [source](#stmt-tr-lipschitz)): Let $\mathcal M$ be a von Neumann algebra with a finite normal trace $T$, let $H=H^*\in\mathcal M$, and let $B\in\mathcal M$. If $f$ is real-valued and Lipschitz on an interval containing the spectrum of $H$, then $$\\|[f(H),B]\\|_{2,T} \leq\operatorname{Lip}(f)\\|[H,B]\\|_{2,T}.$$ Consequently, if $W$ is a finite row, $a=a^*$ and $m=m^*$ are compatible contractions, and $$R=W(1+W^*W)^{-1/2},$$ then $\\|R\\|\leq1$ and $$\\|aR-Rm\\|_{2,T}\leq\\|aW-Wm\\|_{2,T}.$$ The constants do not depend on the size of the matrices or on $\\|W\\|$.
  - Explanation: Lipschitz functional calculus controls commutators in finite tracial norm and regularizes rows without constants depending on their size or operator norm.
- **claim-joining-commuting-cones** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-join)): Let $B$ be a $C^*$-algebra, and let $\phi_1,\phi_2:M_k\to B$ be c.p.c. order-zero maps with commuting ranges. Set $h_i=\phi_i(1)$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\phi:M_k\to C^*(\phi_1(M_k),\phi_2(M_k))$ such that $$\label{loc:join-weight} \phi(1)=1-(1-h_1)(1-h_2)=h_1+h_2-h_1h_2.$$ Its weight dominates both $h_1$ and $h_2$. If both original maps commute with a subset of an ambient algebra, the joined map also commutes with that subset.
  - Explanation: Commuting matrix cones can be joined inside their generated algebra so that the new weight dominates each old weight and retains existing commutation relations.
- **claim-uniform-bounded-trace-filling** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-bounded-fill)): Let $D\subseteq A$ be hereditary, $k\ge2$, $F\subset D$ finite, and $\mathcal P\subset D$ a finite set of positive contractions. For every $\rho>0$ there is a c.p.c. map $\mu:M_k\to D$ such that, for all matrix units $e_{ij},e_{rs}$ and all $x\in F$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|[\mu(e_{ij}),x]\\|_{2,T_{\leq1}(D)}&<\rho,\label{loc:fill-comm}\\ \\|\mu(e_{ij})\mu(e_{rs})-\mu(1)\mu(e_{ij}e_{rs}) \\|_{2,T_{\leq1}(D)}&<\rho,\label{loc:fill-oz}\\ \sigma\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-\mu(1))a^{1/2}\bigr)&<\rho \quad(a\in\mathcal P,\ \sigma\in T_{\leq1}(D)). \label{loc:fill-trace} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Repeated joining geometrically decreases trace defects, yielding finite-coordinate approximate centrality, order-zero identities and small deficits uniformly over bounded traces.
- **claim-relative-trace-sigma-ideal** (construction, [source](#stmt-loc-sigma-ideal)): Definition <a href="#loc:relative-ideal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:relative-ideal">33</a> is independent of the bounded representative. It defines a closed two-sided ideal of $A_\omega$. Moreover, for every separable $C^*$-subalgebra $C_0\subseteq A_\omega$ there is a positive contraction $e\in J_b\cap C_0'$ such that $ey=y$ for every $y\in J_b\cap C_0$.
  - Explanation: Spectral rank cuts define a representative-independent closed ideal whose absorbing contractions can commute with any prescribed separable subalgebra.
- **claim-localized-norm-central-trace-filling** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-localized-trace)): Let $k\ge2$, $b\in A_+$, and let $a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(b)$ be a positive contraction. Suppose that, for some finite $C>0$, $$\label{loc:relative-rank} d_\tau(a)\le C d_\tau(b)\qquad\text{for every }\tau.$$ There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\phi:M_k\to A_\omega\cap A'$ with weight $h$ such that every positive contractive representative $(h_n)$ of $h$ has the following property: for every $\eta>0$, on a common $\omega$-large set of coordinates, $$\label{loc:TL} \tau\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\bigr) \le\eta d_\tau(b) \qquad(d_\tau(b)<\infty).$$ In particular the left side is exactly zero when $d_\tau(b)=0$.* *The finite-coordinate form permits arbitrary relative trace accuracies together with prescribed finite norm tests. Given a finite set $F\subset A$, finitely many pairs $(a_l,b_l)$ satisfying <a href="#loc:relative-rank" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="loc:relative-rank">[loc:relative-rank]</a> with finite constants $C_l$, positive accuracies $\eta_l$, and $\rho>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $\theta:M_k\to A$ whose matrix-unit order-zero defects and commutators with $F$ have norm less than $\rho$, and whose weight satisfies $$\label{loc:TL-coordinate} \tau\bigl(a_l^{1/2}(1-\theta(1))a_l^{1/2}\bigr) \le\eta_l d_\tau(b_l) \qquad(d_\tau(b_l)<\infty)$$ for every $l$, simultaneously over all the indicated traces.
  - Explanation: Hereditary localization and removal of the relative trace ideal produce norm-central cones with arbitrarily small relative trace deficits, including exact zero at vanishing controlling rank and simultaneous finite-coordinate tests.
- **claim-coordinate-ideal-visibility** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-idealvisibility)): For every fixed $k\ge2$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\nu:M_k\to A_\omega\cap A'$, represented by c.p.c. maps $\nu_n:M_k\to A$, with weights $h_n^\nu=\nu_n(1)$, such that the following holds. For every test $(a,\varepsilon)$, put $$b=(a-\varepsilon/8)_+,\qquad y_n^\nu=a^{1/2}g(h_n^\nu)^2a^{1/2}.$$ There are integers $L_{a,\varepsilon,n}\ge1$ and an $\omega$-large set $E_{a,\varepsilon}$ on which $$\label{cmp:I} [b]\le L_{a,\varepsilon,n}[(y_n^\nu-\varepsilon/20)_+] \quad\text{in }\mathrm{Cu}(A).$$ The integers need not be bounded as $n$ varies.
  - Explanation: Coordinate norm visibility gives exact ideal cut comparisons with finite multiplicities that may grow with the coordinate.
- **claim-adaptive-rank-gap-witnesses** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-rankgap)): For every fixed $k\ge2$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\zeta^k:M_k\to A_\omega\cap A'$ with weight $H$ such that, for every test $(a,\varepsilon)$, if $$\label{cmp:u-y} u^k(a)=(1-f(H))a, \qquad y^k(a)=g(\zeta^k)(e_{11})^2a,$$ there is $r_{a,\varepsilon}^k\in A_\omega$ satisfying $$\label{cmp:bounded-comparison} (u^k(a)-\varepsilon)_+ =(r_{a,\varepsilon}^k)^*y^k(a)r_{a,\varepsilon}^k, \qquad \\|r_{a,\varepsilon}^k\\|^2\le32/\varepsilon.$$ In particular the witness bound is independent of $k$.
  - Explanation: Tracial accuracies are chosen after coordinate ideal multiplicities. Exact coordinate comparisons and spectral margins yield bounded defect-to-corner witnesses independently of matrix size.
- **claim-countably-many-central-slots** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-slots)): For every $p\ge2$ there are a c.p.c. order-zero map $\alpha:M_p\to A_\omega\cap A'$, with weight $h=\alpha(1)$, and contractions $s_j\in A_\omega\cap A'$ for $j\ge1$ such that, putting $P=s_1$, one has $$\label{cmp:slot-relations} P\ge0,\qquad s_i^*s_j=\delta_{ij}P^2, \qquad \alpha(e_{11})s_j=s_j.$$ For every test $(a,\varepsilon)$ there is $r_{a,\varepsilon}\in A_\omega$ with $$\label{cmp:slot-comparison} ((1-h)a-\varepsilon)_+ =r_{a,\varepsilon}^*(P^2a)r_{a,\varepsilon}, \qquad \\|r_{a,\varepsilon}\\|^2\le64/\varepsilon.$$ No centrality is asserted or needed for these comparison witnesses.
  - Explanation: Uniform individual witness bounds permit diagonalization across growing matrix sizes, producing orthogonal equivalent central slots and exact cut-defect comparisons. The witnesses need not be central.
- **claim-positive-elementary-tensor-in-ideal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cen-elementary-tensor)): Let $D$ and $C$ be $C^*$-algebras. Every nonzero closed two-sided ideal of $D\otimes C$ contains a nonzero tensor $a\otimes b$ with $a\in D_+$ and $b\in C_+$.
  - Explanation: Every nonzero ideal of a spatial tensor product contains a nonzero positive elementary tensor, by pure-state excision and tensor cut calculus.
- **claim-nuclear-tensor-ideal-generation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cen-nuclear-tensor-ideals)): If $D$ is nuclear and $C$ is arbitrary, every closed two-sided ideal $J\subset D\otimes C$ is generated by the positive elementary tensors that it contains.
  - Explanation: Nuclearity of a tensor factor allows arbitrary tensor-product ideals to be generated by their contained positive elementary tensors.
- **claim-simultaneous-weighted-domination** (construction, [source](#stmt-cen-weighted-domination)): Let $D\subseteq E$ be a nuclear $C^*$-subalgebra of a $C^*$-algebra $E$, and let $v,w$ be positive contractions in $\widetilde E\cap D'$. Put $\Phi(a)=va$ and $\Psi(a)=wa$ for $a\in D$; these products belong to $E$. Suppose that $$\Phi(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(a))\qquad(a\in D_+).$$ Then for every finite set $F\subset D$ and every $\delta>0$, there are an integer $N\ge1$ and $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in E$ such that $$\left\\|va-\sum_{j=1}^N v_j^*wa v_j\right\\|<\delta \qquad(a\in F).$$
  - Explanation: Pointwise ideal containment for weighted inclusions gives a common finite list of compression coefficients approximating every prescribed finite test, using tensor ideal generation and separation.
- **claim-bounded-central-defect-filler** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-central-filler)): Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>. For each $p\ge2$ there are a c.p.c. order-zero map $\alpha:M_p\to A_\omega\cap A'$, with $h=\alpha(1)$, and an element $S\in A_\omega\cap A'$ such that $$\label{cen:filler-relations} \alpha(e_{11})S=S,\qquad S^*S a=(1-h)a\quad(a\in A).$$ One can choose $\\|S\\|\le2$. In the second equation the expression $(1-h)a$ is computed in the unitization of $A_\omega$ and belongs to $A_\omega$.
  - Explanation: Central slots pack simultaneous compressions. Approximate-unit tests bound the packed element and its commutators, permitting a central defect filler in the ultrapower.
- **claim-dimension-drop-presentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-dimensiondrop)): Let $B$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $p\ge2$, and suppose that a c.p.c. order-zero map $\alpha:M_p\to B$ and $s\in B$ satisfy $$h=\alpha(1),\qquad s^*s=1-h,\qquad \alpha(e_{11})s=s.$$ There is a unital homomorphism $I(p,p+1)\to B$ whose range contains $\alpha(M_p)$ and $s$. Consequently $F_\omega(A)$ receives a unital homomorphism from $I(p,p+1)$ for every $p\ge2$.
  - Explanation: The matrix-cone defect relations have an explicit dimension-drop model through which every representation factors, giving a unital homomorphism whose range contains the given generators.
- **claim-prime-block-amplification** (construction, [source](#stmt-abs-prime-block-map)): If $r$ and $s$ are relatively prime, there is a unital homomorphism $I(r,s)\to I(p,p+1)$ for some $p\ge2$.
  - Explanation: Compatible congruences produce matrix amplifications preserving the endpoint conditions and giving a unital homomorphism into a consecutive-size dimension-drop block.
- **claim-nuclear-approximate-retraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-abs-approximate-retraction)): Let $D\subset L$ be a unital inclusion with $D$ nuclear. Given a finite subset $G\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $R:L\to D$ with $\\|R(d)-d\\|<\varepsilon$ for $d\in G$.
  - Explanation: Nuclear approximation and completely positive extension yield an approximate retraction on any prescribed finite subset; exact unitality is unnecessary.
- **claim-unital-central-jiang-su-map** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-z-map)): Let $A$ be a nonzero separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>, and fix any free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. Then there is a unital homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$.
  - Explanation: Approximate retractions to prime blocks and bounded completely positive lifts produce a single unital Jiang–Su homomorphism in the annihilator quotient without compatibility of the original block maps.
- **claim-connected-unitaries-and-lifting** (theorem, [source](#stmt-abs-connected-unitaries)): The unitary groups of $\mathcal Z$ and $\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z$ are path connected. If $q:D\to D/J$ is a unital quotient map, every unitary in the identity component of $\mathcal U(D/J)$ lifts to a unitary in $D$.
  - Explanation: Prime-block winding arguments and inductive limits establish connectedness of Jiang–Su unitary groups; products of lifted selfadjoint exponentials lift identity-component quotient unitaries.
- **claim-finite-unitary-absorption-criterion** (construction, [source](#stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion)): Let $A$ be a separable $C^*$-algebra, and suppose there is a unital homomorphism $\varphi:\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. For finite sets $F\subset A$, $G\subset A\otimes\mathcal Z$, and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a unitary $u\in A^{\sim}\otimes\mathcal Z$ such that $$\label{abs:unitary-criterion} \\|[u,a\otimes1]\\|<\varepsilon\quad(a\in F),\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(u^*bu,A\otimes1)<\varepsilon\quad(b\in G).$$
  - Explanation: The central Jiang–Su map and connected half-flip lifts yield multiplier unitaries approximately commuting with domain tests and conjugating target tests near the first-factor algebra.
- **claim-central-map-implies-absorption** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-absorption)): If a separable $C^*$-algebra $A$ admits a unital homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$, then $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$.
  - Explanation: A unital central Jiang–Su homomorphism implies absorption by a summable point-norm intertwining with stored target preimages. This implication requires separability but not nuclearity of the algebra.

```json
{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-thm-main",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-abstract-cu",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra.         "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-abstract-cu",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra.           "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.*\n\n1.  *If $a,b\\in D_+$ and $\\|a-r^*br\\|<\\epsilon$, then there is $v\\in D$ with $$v^*v=(a-\\epsilon)_+,\\qquad vv^*\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b).$$ Consequently, if $a\\precsim b$, the hereditary algebra of every positive cut of $a$ can be placed in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ by a polar isometry.*\n\n2.  *If $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}(b)_+$, then for every $\\epsilon>0$ there is a finite integer $L$ such that $$[(a-\\epsilon)_+]\\le L[b].$$ If $[a]\\le L[b]$, every positive cut of $a$ still compares to $L[(b-\\delta)_+]$ for a sufficiently small $\\delta>0$.*\n\n3.  *Comparisons between elements of a hereditary subalgebra can be witnessed in that subalgebra. Comparisons between specified matrix corners can be witnessed by the appropriate rectangular matrices.*\n\n4.  *If $u\\precsim(y-\\beta)_+$, where $0\\le\\alpha<\\beta$, then for each $\\eta>0$ there is $r\\in D$ such that $$\\|u-r^*(y-\\alpha)_+r\\|<\\eta,\\qquad\n     \\|r\\|^2\\le\\frac{\\|u\\|+\\eta}{\\beta-\\alpha}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.*\n\n1.  *If $a,b\\in D_+$ and $\\|a-r^*br\\|<\\epsilon$, then there is $v\\in D$ with $$v^*v=(a-\\epsilon)_+,\\qquad vv^*\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b).$$ Consequently, if $a\\precsim b$, the hereditary algebra of every positive cut of $a$ can be placed in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ by a polar isometry.*\n\n2.  *If $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}(b)_+$, then for every $\\epsilon>0$ there is a finite integer $L$ such that $$[(a-\\epsilon)_+]\\le L[b].$$ If $[a]\\le L[b]$, every positive cut of $a$ still compares to $L[(b-\\delta)_+]$ for a sufficiently small $\\delta>0$.*\n\n3.  *Comparisons between elements of a hereditary subalgebra can be witnessed in that subalgebra. Comparisons between specified matrix corners can be witnessed by the appropriate rectangular matrices.*\n\n4.  *If $u\\precsim(y-\\beta)_+$, where $0\\le\\alpha<\\beta$, then for each $\\eta>0$ there is $r\\in D$ such that $$\\|u-r^*(y-\\alpha)_+r\\|<\\eta,\\qquad\n     \\|r\\|^2\\le\\frac{\\|u\\|+\\eta}{\\beta-\\alpha}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-placement",
        "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#eq:pre:au\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pre:au\">[eq:pre:au]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:pre:div\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pre:div\">[eq:pre:div]</a>.*\n\n1.  *Let $B$ be a hereditary subalgebra of a matrix algebra over $A\\otimes\\mathcal K$, let $b\\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $N\\ge1$ and $\\epsilon>0$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_N\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$ such that $$\\|\\sigma(\\psi(1))\\|=1\n     \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad\n     \\sigma((b-\\epsilon)_+)\\ne0.$$ Its $N$ diagonal weights are orthogonal and equivalent in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$. Each diagonal weight, cut at $1/2$, dominates a positive class $u_0$ for which $$[(b-\\epsilon)_+]\\le(N+1)u_0.$$*\n\n2.  *Let $C$ be isomorphic to a finite direct sum of hereditary subalgebras as above. For $b\\in C_+$ a contraction, $k\\ge1$, and $0<q\\le1$, there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_k\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_C(b)$, with weight $t=\\psi(1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:pre:P}\n     d_\\sigma(t)\\le q\\,d_\\sigma(b),\\qquad\n     d_\\sigma((t-\\tfrac12)_+)\n           \\ge(q/3)d_\\sigma((b-\\tfrac12)_+)$$ for every bounded positive trace $\\sigma$ on $C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

Each diagonal weight, cut at $1/2$, dominates a positive class $u_0$ for which $$[(b-\\epsilon)_+]\\le(N+1)u_0.$$*\n\n2.  *Let $C$ be isomorphic to a finite direct sum of hereditary subalgebras as above. For $b\\in C_+$ a contraction, $k\\ge1$, and $0<q\\le1$, there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_k\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_C(b)$, with weight $t=\\psi(1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:pre:P}\n     d_\\sigma(t)\\le q\\,d_\\sigma(b),\\qquad\n     d_\\sigma((t-\\tfrac12)_+)\n           \\ge(q/3)d_\\sigma((b-\\tfrac12)_+)$$ for every bounded positive trace $\\sigma$ on $C$.",

There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_N\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$ such that $$\\|\\sigma(\\psi(1))\\|=1\n     \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad\n     \\sigma((b-\\epsilon)_+)\\ne0.$$ Its $N$ diagonal weights are orthogonal and equivalent in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$. Each diagonal weight, cut at $1/2$, dominates a positive class $u_0$ for which $$[(b-\\epsilon)_+]\\le(N+1)u_0.$$*\n\n2.  *Let $C$ be isomorphic to a finite direct sum of hereditary subalgebras as above. For $b\\in C_+$ a contraction, $k\\ge1$, and $0<q\\le1$, there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_k\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_C(b)$, with weight $t=\\psi(1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:pre:P}\n     d_\\sigma(t)\\le q\\,d_\\sigma(b),\\qquad\n     d_\\sigma((t-\\tfrac12)_+)\n           \\ge(q/3)d_\\sigma((b-\\tfrac12)_+)$$ for every bounded positive trace $\\sigma$ on $C$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-diagonal",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a countable family of norm-continuous algebraic and functional-calculus conditions, with fixed coefficients in a norm ultrapower, is approximately satisfiable on every finite subfamily. Suppose each unknown has a fixed individual norm bound in these finite solutions. Then the conditions can be satisfied exactly in the ultrapower. Additional countably many commutator conditions and conditions after multiplication by a fixed countable subset of the algebra may be imposed in the same way.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-shrinking",
        "exact_quote": "There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. If $a,b\\in A$ are positive contractions and $z\\in\\widetilde A$ satisfies $\\|p_a z p_b\\|\\le1$, there are positive contractions $a'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(a)$ and $b'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ such that $$\\label{shr:conclusion}\n \\|p_{a'}z p_{b'}\\|\\le c$$ and, for every irreducible representation $\\sigma$ of $A$, $$\\label{shr:visibility}\n \\max\\{\\|\\sigma(a)\\|,\\|\\sigma(b)\\|\\}\\ge\\tfrac12\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\sigma(a')\\ne0\\ \\text{or}\\ \\sigma(b')\\ne0.$$ The support norms are computed in the bidual, or equivalently in the universal representation.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-shrinking",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. If $a,b\\in A$ are positive contractions and $z\\in\\widetilde A$ satisfies $\\|p_a z p_b\\|\\le1$, there are positive contractions $a'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(a)$ and $b'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ such that $$\\label{shr:conclusion}\n \\|p_{a'}z p_{b'}\\|\\le c$$ and, for every irreducible representation $\\sigma$ of $A$, $$\\label{shr:visibility}\n \\max\\{\\|\\sigma(a)\\|,\\|\\sigma(b)\\|\\}\\ge\\tfrac12\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\sigma(a')\\ne0\\ \\text{or}\\ \\sigma(b')\\ne0.$$ The support norms are computed in the bidual, or equivalently in the universal representation.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>.       "explanation": "A universal contraction factor reduces cross-compression while preserving nonvanishing in each triggered irreducible representation. The proof establishes existence rather than an explicit value of the factor.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A$ satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. Let $B\\subseteq A$ be hereditary, let $D\\in M_l(B)_+$ and $E\\in B_+$, and suppose that $w_1,\\ldots,w_m\\in M_{l,1}(B)$ satisfy $$w_iw_i^*\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(D),\\qquad w_i^*w_i\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(E).$$ For each $\\delta>0$ there is a contraction $v\\in M_{l,1}(B)$ such that $$vv^*\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(D),\\qquad v^*v\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(E),$$ and, in every representation $\\sigma$ of $B$, $$\\max_{1\\le i\\le m}\\|\\sigma(w_i)\\|\\ge\\delta\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad \\|\\sigma(v)\\|=1.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-norm",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A$ is separable and satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-norm-finite-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x\\in A_+$, $\\gamma>0$, and let $(s_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\Lambda}$ be positive elements of $A$. Suppose that $\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)\\ne\\varnothing$ and that, for every $\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$, some $\\sigma(s_\\alpha)$ is nonzero. Then there are $\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_m$ and $t>0$ such that $$\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\max_{1\\le i\\le m}\\|\\sigma(s_{\\alpha_i})\\|>t.$$ Thus the nonvanishing loci of the cuts $(s_{\\alpha_i}-t)_+$ still cover $\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$. For a finite covering family of positive contractions, continuous functional calculus can replace its members by positive contractions with the same supports and with maximum represented norm equal to one at every $\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-norm-prototype",
        "exact_quote": "For this finite word model there is a positive contractive seed $s\\in A$ with $\\sigma(s)\\ne0$, whose support $P=p_s$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n PxP&\\ge(\\gamma/4)P,                                      \\label{norm:positive-compression}\\\\\n \\big\\|(Pb_i^*b_jP)_{i,j=1}^m-I_m\\otimes P\\big\\|\n      &\\le\\eta^2/4,                                     \\label{norm:local-gram}\\\\\n \\big\\|(Pr_i(a)^*r_j(a)P)_{l_i,l_j<d}\\big\\|\n      &\\le\\eta^2/4\\qquad(a\\in F).                        \\label{norm:local-residual}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every positive element in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(s)$ has a support on which the same three estimates hold.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-norm-prototype",

          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For this finite word model there is a positive contractive seed $s\\in A$ with $\\sigma(s)\\ne0$, whose support $P=p_s$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n PxP&\\ge(\\gamma/4)P,                                      \\label{norm:positive-compression}\\\\\n \\big\\|(Pb_i^*b_jP)_{i,j=1}^m-I_m\\otimes P\\big\\|\n      &\\le\\eta^2/4,                                     \\label{norm:local-gram}\\\\\n \\big\\|(Pr_i(a)^*r_j(a)P)_{l_i,l_j<d}\\big\\|\n      &\\le\\eta^2/4\\qquad(a\\in F).                        \\label{norm:local-residual}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Every positive element in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(s)$ has a support on which the same three estimates hold.",

        "exact_quote": "Assume that $A$ satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-norm-finite-stage",
        "exact_quote": "Assume that $A$ satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. Let $x\\in A$ be a positive contraction, $\\gamma>0$, $k\\ge2$, $F\\subset A$ a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, $d\\ge1$, and $0<\\eta<1$.         "source_id": "PM-291.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\geq2$ and put $$L=(20k)^2,\\qquad C_k=1+4L.$$ Let $F\\subset D$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, let $\\mathcal P\\subseteq F$ consist of positive contractions, and let $0<\\delta<1$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $V:M_k\\to D$ such that, for every $\\tau\\in T_{\\leq1}(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\|x\\|\\leq1}\\|[a,V(x)]\\|_{2,\\tau}\n &<\\varepsilon &&(a\\in F),\\label{tr:central-target}\\\\\n \\tau(aV(1))\n &\\geq \\frac\\delta3\\tau(a)-C_k\\delta^{3/2}-\\varepsilon\n &&(a\\in\\mathcal P).\\label{tr:mass-target}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $k$ is fixed before the accuracy is chosen; no bound on the number of nuclear approximation summands is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-tr-lipschitz",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal M$ be a von Neumann algebra with a finite normal trace $T$, let $H=H^*\\in\\mathcal M$, and let $B\\in\\mathcal M$. If $f$ is real-valued and Lipschitz on an interval containing the spectrum of $H$, then $$\\|[f(H),B]\\|_{2,T}\n \\leq\\operatorname{Lip}(f)\\|[H,B]\\|_{2,T}.$$ Consequently, if $W$ is a finite row, $a=a^*$ and $m=m^*$ are compatible contractions, and $$R=W(1+W^*W)^{-1/2},$$ then $\\|R\\|\\leq1$ and $$\\|aR-Rm\\|_{2,T}\\leq\\|aW-Wm\\|_{2,T}.$$ The constants do not depend on the size of the matrices or on $\\|W\\|$.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-tr-lipschitz",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal M$ be a von Neumann algebra with a finite normal trace $T$, let $H=H^*\\in\\mathcal M$, and let $B\\in\\mathcal M$. If $f$ is real-valued and Lipschitz on an interval containing the spectrum of $H$, then $$\\|[f(H),B]\\|_{2,T}\n \\leq\\operatorname{Lip}(f)\\|[H,B]\\|_{2,T}.$$ Consequently, if $W$ is a finite row, $a=a^*$ and $m=m^*$ are compatible contractions, and $$R=W(1+W^*W)^{-1/2},$$ then $\\|R\\|\\leq1$ and $$\\|aR-Rm\\|_{2,T}\\leq\\|aW-Wm\\|_{2,T}.$$ The constants do not depend on the size of the matrices or on $\\|W\\|$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal M$ be a von Neumann algebra with a finite normal trace $T$, let $H=H^*\\in\\mathcal M$, and let $B\\in\\mathcal M$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-join",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a $C^*$-algebra, and let $\\phi_1,\\phi_2:M_k\\to B$ be c.p.c. order-zero maps with commuting ranges. Set $h_i=\\phi_i(1)$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to\nC^*(\\phi_1(M_k),\\phi_2(M_k))$ such that $$\\label{loc:join-weight}\n \\phi(1)=1-(1-h_1)(1-h_2)=h_1+h_2-h_1h_2.$$ Its weight dominates both $h_1$ and $h_2$. If both original maps commute with a subset of an ambient algebra, the joined map also commutes with that subset.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-bounded-fill",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subseteq A$ be hereditary, $k\\ge2$, $F\\subset D$ finite, and $\\mathcal P\\subset D$ a finite set of positive contractions. For every $\\rho>0$ there is a c.p.c. map $\\mu:M_k\\to D$ such that, for all matrix units $e_{ij},e_{rs}$ and all $x\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|[\\mu(e_{ij}),x]\\|_{2,T_{\\leq1}(D)}&<\\rho,\\label{loc:fill-comm}\\\\\n \\|\\mu(e_{ij})\\mu(e_{rs})-\\mu(1)\\mu(e_{ij}e_{rs})\n       \\|_{2,T_{\\leq1}(D)}&<\\rho,\\label{loc:fill-oz}\\\\\n \\sigma\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-\\mu(1))a^{1/2}\\bigr)&<\\rho\n       \\quad(a\\in\\mathcal P,\\ \\sigma\\in T_{\\leq1}(D)).\n       \\label{loc:fill-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-loc-bounded-fill",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subseteq A$ be hereditary, $k\\ge2$, $F\\subset D$ finite, and $\\mathcal P\\subset D$ a finite set of positive contractions.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-sigma-ideal",
        "exact_quote": "Definition <a href=\"#loc:relative-ideal\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"loc:relative-ideal\">33</a> is independent of the bounded representative.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-localized-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, $b\\in A_+$, and let $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(b)$ be a positive contraction. Suppose that, for some finite $C>0$, $$\\label{loc:relative-rank}\n d_\\tau(a)\\le C d_\\tau(b)\\qquad\\text{for every }\\tau.$$ There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $h$ such that every positive contractive representative $(h_n)$ of $h$ has the following property: for every $\\eta>0$, on a common $\\omega$-large set of coordinates, $$\\label{loc:TL}\n \\tau\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\\bigr)\n       \\le\\eta d_\\tau(b)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b)<\\infty).$$ In particular the left side is exactly zero when $d_\\tau(b)=0$.*\n\n\n*The finite-coordinate form permits arbitrary relative trace accuracies together with prescribed finite norm tests. Given a finite set $F\\subset A$, finitely many pairs $(a_l,b_l)$ satisfying <a href=\"#loc:relative-rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:relative-rank\">[loc:relative-rank]</a> with finite constants $C_l$, positive accuracies $\\eta_l$, and $\\rho>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $\\theta:M_k\\to A$ whose matrix-unit order-zero defects and commutators with $F$ have norm less than $\\rho$, and whose weight satisfies $$\\label{loc:TL-coordinate}\n \\tau\\bigl(a_l^{1/2}(1-\\theta(1))a_l^{1/2}\\bigr)\n     \\le\\eta_l d_\\tau(b_l)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b_l)<\\infty)$$ for every $l$, simultaneously over all the indicated traces.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-localized-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, $b\\in A_+$, and let $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(b)$ be a positive contraction. Suppose that, for some finite $C>0$, $$\\label{loc:relative-rank}\n d_\\tau(a)\\le C d_\\tau(b)\\qquad\\text{for every }\\tau.$$ There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $h$ such that every positive contractive representative $(h_n)$ of $h$ has the following property: for every $\\eta>0$, on a common $\\omega$-large set of coordinates, $$\\label{loc:TL}\n \\tau\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\\bigr)\n       \\le\\eta d_\\tau(b)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b)<\\infty).$$ In particular the left side is exactly zero when $d_\\tau(b)=0$.*\n\n\n*The finite-coordinate form permits arbitrary relative trace accuracies together with prescribed finite norm tests. Given a finite set $F\\subset A$, finitely many pairs $(a_l,b_l)$ satisfying <a href=\"#loc:relative-rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:relative-rank\">[loc:relative-rank]</a> with finite constants $C_l$, positive accuracies $\\eta_l$, and $\\rho>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $\\theta:M_k\\to A$ whose matrix-unit order-zero defects and commutators with $F$ have norm less than $\\rho$, and whose weight satisfies $$\\label{loc:TL-coordinate}\n \\tau\\bigl(a_l^{1/2}(1-\\theta(1))a_l^{1/2}\\bigr)\n     \\le\\eta_l d_\\tau(b_l)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b_l)<\\infty)$$ for every $l$, simultaneously over all the indicated traces.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

For every test $(a,\\varepsilon)$, put $$b=(a-\\varepsilon/8)_+,\\qquad\n y_n^\\nu=a^{1/2}g(h_n^\\nu)^2a^{1/2}.$$ There are integers $L_{a,\\varepsilon,n}\\ge1$ and an $\\omega$-large set $E_{a,\\varepsilon}$ on which $$\\label{cmp:I}\n [b]\\le L_{a,\\varepsilon,n}[(y_n^\\nu-\\varepsilon/20)_+]\n          \\quad\\text{in }\\mathrm{Cu}(A).$$ The integers need not be bounded as $n$ varies.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-rankgap",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $k\\ge2$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\zeta^k:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $H$ such that, for every test $(a,\\varepsilon)$, if $$\\label{cmp:u-y}\n u^k(a)=(1-f(H))a,\n \\qquad y^k(a)=g(\\zeta^k)(e_{11})^2a,$$ there is $r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k\\in A_\\omega$ satisfying $$\\label{cmp:bounded-comparison}\n (u^k(a)-\\varepsilon)_+\n   =(r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k)^*y^k(a)r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k,\n \\qquad \\|r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k\\|^2\\le32/\\varepsilon.$$ In particular the witness bound is independent of $k$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-slots",
        "exact_quote": "For every $p\\ge2$ there are a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\alpha:M_p\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$, with weight $h=\\alpha(1)$, and contractions $s_j\\in A_\\omega\\cap A'$ for $j\\ge1$ such that, putting $P=s_1$, one has $$\\label{cmp:slot-relations}\n P\\ge0,\\qquad s_i^*s_j=\\delta_{ij}P^2,\n \\qquad \\alpha(e_{11})s_j=s_j.$$ For every test $(a,\\varepsilon)$ there is $r_{a,\\varepsilon}\\in A_\\omega$ with $$\\label{cmp:slot-comparison}\n ((1-h)a-\\varepsilon)_+\n       =r_{a,\\varepsilon}^*(P^2a)r_{a,\\varepsilon},\n \\qquad \\|r_{a,\\varepsilon}\\|^2\\le64/\\varepsilon.$$ No centrality is asserted or needed for these comparison witnesses.",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-slots",

          "source_id": "PM-291.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-cen-weighted-domination",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subseteq E$ be a nuclear $C^*$-subalgebra of a $C^*$-algebra $E$, and let $v,w$ be positive contractions in $\\widetilde E\\cap D'$. Put $\\Phi(a)=va$ and $\\Psi(a)=wa$ for $a\\in D$; these products belong to $E$. Suppose that $$\\Phi(a)\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\\Psi(a))\\qquad(a\\in D_+).$$ Then for every finite set $F\\subset D$ and every $\\delta>0$, there are an integer $N\\ge1$ and $v_1,\\ldots,v_N\\in E$ such that $$\\left\\|va-\\sum_{j=1}^N v_j^*wa v_j\\right\\|<\\delta\n \\qquad(a\\in F).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-291.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Cuntz subequivalence** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For $a,b\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, write $a\precsim b$ if $v_j^*bv_j\to a$ in norm for some sequence $(v_j)$ in $A\otimes\mathcal K$.
- **Cuntz classes** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Mutual comparison defines the Cuntz classes $[a]$.
- **Cuntz-semigroup functional** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *functional* on $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ is a map to $[0,\infty]$ preserving zero, addition, order, and increasing suprema.
- **Extended-functional strict comparison** ([source](#ext:eq:comparison)): We use the following precise extended-functional convention for *strict comparison*: for every $x,y\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$, $$\label{ext:eq:comparison} \left. \begin{aligned} \varphi(x)&\le\varphi(y) &&\text{for every }\varphi\in\mathrm F(\mathrm{Cu}(A)),\\ \varphi(x)&<\varphi(y) &&\text{whenever }0<\varphi(y)<\infty \end{aligned} \right\} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\le y.$$ The second line concerns the same cone of functionals. Arithmetic at zero and infinity is extended nonnegative arithmetic; in particular, strict inequality is required precisely at positive finite target values. An algebra is *non-elementary* if it is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space.
- **Non-elementary algebra** ([source](#ext:eq:comparison)): An algebra is *non-elementary* if it is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space.
- **Almost unperforation** ([source](#eq:pre:au)): The first order property we require is *almost unperforation*: $$(l+1)x\le ly\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\le y \qquad(x,y\in\mathrm{Cu}(A),\ l\ge1). \label{eq:pre:au}$$
- **Full almost divisibility** ([source](#eq:pre:div)): The second is *full almost divisibility*: for every $x\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and integer $N\ge1$, there is $u\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ such that $$Nu\le x\le(N+1)u. \label{eq:pre:div}$$
- **Condition (C)** ([source](#eq:pre:div)): We call the conjunction of these two properties <span class="upright">(C)</span>.
- **Hereditary algebra, generated ideal and support projection** ([source](#sec:preliminaries)): For $b\in D_+$, set $$\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_D(b)=\overline{bDb},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(b)=\overline{\operatorname{span}}(DbD),\qquad p_b=1_{(0,\infty)}(b)\in D^{**}.$$
- **Order-zero map** ([source](#pm-h-8)): It has *order zero* if it preserves orthogonality of positive elements.
- **Supporting homomorphism and weight** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\theta:E\to D$ with unital domain, the order-zero structure theorem [[WZ2009](#ref-WZ2009)] gives $$\theta(z)=h\rho(z)=\rho(z)h,\qquad h=\theta(1),\qquad \rho(1)=p_h,$$ where $\rho:E\to D^{**}$ is a supporting homomorphism.
- **Matrix cone** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A *matrix cone* means a c.p.c. order-zero map from $M_k$, for some $k$.
- **Extended trace rank** ([source](#eq:pre:trace-rank)): For $b\ge0$, write $$d_\tau(b)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\tau(b^{1/j}),$$ first rescaling to a contraction when necessary.
- **Functionals finite somewhere** ([source](#ext:eq:controller)): Write $\mathrm F_{\mathrm{fin}}(A)$ for the third type in Lemma <a href="#ext:lem:trichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:lem:trichotomy">8</a>.
- **Norm ultrapower** ([source](#pm-h-20)): We write $$D_\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,D)/ \{(x_n):\lim_{n\to\omega}\\|x_n\\|=0\},$$ and identify $D$ with its constant copy.
- **Central annihilator quotient** ([source](#pm-h-34)): Put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A)=\{x\in A_\omega:xa=ax=0\text{ for every }a\in A\}, \qquad F_\omega(A)=(A_\omega\cap A')/\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A).$$
- **Representation visibility locus** ([source](#pm-h-23)): For $x\in A_+$ and $\gamma>0$, write $$\mathcal K(x,\gamma) =\{\sigma:\sigma\text{ is an irreducible representation of }A,\ \\|\sigma(x)\\|\ge\gamma\}.$$
- **Relative trace ideal** ([source](#stmt-loc-relative-ideal)): For $b\in A_+$ define $J_b\subseteq A_\omega$ as follows. An element $z=(z_n)$ belongs to $J_b$ if, for every $s>0$ and $\eta>0$, there is $E\in\omega$ such that $$\label{loc:J-predicate} d_\tau((\|z_n\|-s)_+)\le\eta d_\tau(b) \quad\text{for every }n\in E\text{ and every }\tau \text{ with }d_\tau(b)<\infty.$$ For $d_\tau(b)=0$ this means exact zero on the left. The set $E$ is common to the entire indicated trace family. It is enough to test positive rational $s,\eta$.
- **Dimension-drop algebra** ([source](#pm-h-35)): For positive integers $r,s$, write $$I(r,s)=\{f\in C([0,1],M_r\otimes M_s): f(0)\in M_r\otimes1_s,\ f(1)\in1_r\otimes M_s\}.$$

```json
          "exact_quote": "We use the following precise extended-functional convention for *strict comparison*: for every $x,y\\in\\mathrm{Cu}(A)$, $$\\label{ext:eq:comparison}\n\\left.\n\\begin{aligned}\n \\varphi(x)&\\le\\varphi(y)\n       &&\\text{for every }\\varphi\\in\\mathrm F(\\mathrm{Cu}(A)),\\\\\n \\varphi(x)&<\\varphi(y)\n       &&\\text{whenever }0<\\varphi(y)<\\infty\n\\end{aligned}\n\\right\\}\n\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad x\\le y.$$ The second line concerns the same cone of functionals. Arithmetic at zero and infinity is extended nonnegative arithmetic; in particular, strict inequality is required precisely at positive finite target values. An algebra is *non-elementary* if it is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
```

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-support-rank-rounding-error | Spectral-rounding excess of trace over cut rank | ≤ | $d_\tau(c)$ | The spectral-level integer exceeds the norm of the transferred positive element; the error does not depend on its value. |
| q-relative-summable-error-budget | E | < | $s-\frac1{N+1}$ | The scaling is chosen strictly inside the divisibility interval and the reciprocal support multiplicities are summable. |
| q-functional-dividing-class-upper | \varphi(u) | < | $\frac{\varphi(x)}N$ | The displayed strict interval applies to finite positive functional values; infinite values are handled separately. |
| q-unital-uniform-rank-error | \tau(p)-d_\tau(w) | ≤ | $\eta$ | The matrix trace is unnormalized and the error is simultaneous over the tracial state space. |
| q-rank-gap-comparison | d_\tau(v) | ≤ | $\tfrac12d_\tau(w)$ | The rank inequality includes zero and infinite values. |
| q-two-support-shrinking-factor | c | < | $1$ | The constant is positive and universal over the stated algebras and support data; no explicit value is given. |
| q-coordinate-visibility-threshold | \kappa | = | $\gamma^2/32$ | The positive contraction, detection threshold and matrix size are fixed; the bound is simultaneous over coordinates and representations. |
| q-finite-stage-order-zero-error | \\|V(z)V(w)\\| | ≤ | $\eta^2$ | The product estimate concerns orthogonal positive contractive matrix inputs. |
| q-finite-stage-commutator-error | \\|[a,V(z)]\\| | ≤ | $2\eta+2/d$ | The estimate is uniform over the matrix unit ball and the prescribed finite selfadjoint test set. |
| q-bounded-trace-matrix-constant | C_k | = | $1+4L$ | The matrix size fixes the constants before the accuracy is chosen; approximation summand count is unrestricted. |
| q-bounded-trace-mass-retention | \tau(aV(1)) | ≥ | $\frac\delta3\tau(a)-C_k\delta^{3/2}-\varepsilon$ | The lower bound retains the stated subtractive losses and does not assert positive retained mass for every parameter choice. |
| q-joined-trace-defect | D_N(a) | ≤ | $(1-\delta/3)^N+3C_k\sqrt\delta$ | Choose the fractional parameter before the finite joining count; the estimate includes the residual loss. |
| q-localized-relative-trace-deficit | \tau\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\bigr) | ≤ | $\eta d_\tau(b)$ | A common ultrafilter-large coordinate set works for all traces with finite controlling rank, including exact zero at zero rank. |
| q-adaptive-comparison-witness | \\|r_{a,\varepsilon}^k\\|^2 | ≤ | $32/\varepsilon$ | The bound depends on the cut threshold and is independent of matrix size and coordinate ideal multiplicities. |
| q-central-slot-comparison-witness | \\|r_{a,\varepsilon}\\|^2 | ≤ | $64/\varepsilon$ | Each comparison test has its individual uniform witness bound; centrality of the witness is unnecessary. |
| q-central-filler-norm | \\|S\\| | ≤ | $2$ | The bound applies to the compressed and diagonalized filler, rather than the original unbounded packed sums. |
| q-intertwining-tolerance | \varepsilon_n | = | $2^{-n}$ | The tolerance sequence is summable and controls changes on all stored domain tests. |
| q-intertwining-stored-preimage-tail | \\|\Phi(c_{n,j})-b_j\\| | ≤ | $2^{1-n}$ | Earlier target preimages remain in every subsequent finite domain test set, giving the accumulated tail bound. |

```json
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-shrinking",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. If $a,b\\in A$ are positive contractions and $z\\in\\widetilde A$ satisfies $\\|p_a z p_b\\|\\le1$, there are positive contractions $a'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(a)$ and $b'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ such that $$\\label{shr:conclusion}\n \\|p_{a'}z p_{b'}\\|\\le c$$ and, for every irreducible representation $\\sigma$ of $A$, $$\\label{shr:visibility}\n \\max\\{\\|\\sigma(a)\\|,\\|\\sigma(b)\\|\\}\\ge\\tfrac12\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\sigma(a')\\ne0\\ \\text{or}\\ \\sigma(b')\\ne0.$$ The support norms are computed in the bidual, or equivalently in the universal representation.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-norm",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A$ is separable and satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>.           "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\geq2$ and put $$L=(20k)^2,\\qquad C_k=1+4L.$$ Let $F\\subset D$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, let $\\mathcal P\\subseteq F$ consist of positive contractions, and let $0<\\delta<1$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $V:M_k\\to D$ such that, for every $\\tau\\in T_{\\leq1}(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\|x\\|\\leq1}\\|[a,V(x)]\\|_{2,\\tau}\n &<\\varepsilon &&(a\\in F),\\label{tr:central-target}\\\\\n \\tau(aV(1))\n &\\geq \\frac\\delta3\\tau(a)-C_k\\delta^{3/2}-\\varepsilon\n &&(a\\in\\mathcal P).\\label{tr:mass-target}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $k$ is fixed before the accuracy is chosen; no bound on the number of nuclear approximation summands is required.",

        }

        {
          "anchor": "loc:geometric",
          "exact_quote": "Start with joined weight $H_0=0$. Having constructed the preceding joined map, let $r=1-H_j$ and prescribe the targets $r^{1/2}ar^{1/2}$ for $a\\in\\mathcal P$. Prescribe commutation with the preceding map and with the fixed tests. Apply <a href=\"#loc:fraction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:fraction\">[loc:fraction]</a> and join the new map to the preceding one using Lemma <a href=\"#lem:join\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:join\">31</a>. The new weight commutes with $r$, and hence, for any trace $\\sigma_\\omega$, the defect $$D_j(a)=\\sigma_\\omega\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-H_j)a^{1/2}\\bigr)$$ satisfies $$D_{j+1}(a)\\le(1-\\delta/3)D_j(a)+C_k\\delta^{3/2}.$$ This follows by subtracting $\\sigma_\\omega(r^{1/2}ar^{1/2}\\psi(1))$ from $D_j(a)$; traciality and commutation of $\\psi(1)$ with $r$ justify the subtraction without any commutation assumption on $a$. Since $D_0(a)\\le1$, induction gives $$\\label{loc:geometric}\n D_N(a)\\le(1-\\delta/3)^N+3C_k\\sqrt\\delta.$$ Choose $\\delta$ so that the second term is less than $\\rho/4$, and then a finite $N$ so that the first term is less than $\\rho/4$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-joined-trace-defect",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "D_N(a)",
      "regime": {

        "hypotheses": [

            "anchor": "stmt-loc-bounded-fill",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-localized-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, $b\\in A_+$, and let $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(b)$ be a positive contraction. Suppose that, for some finite $C>0$, $$\\label{loc:relative-rank}\n d_\\tau(a)\\le C d_\\tau(b)\\qquad\\text{for every }\\tau.$$ There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $h$ such that every positive contractive representative $(h_n)$ of $h$ has the following property: for every $\\eta>0$, on a common $\\omega$-large set of coordinates, $$\\label{loc:TL}\n \\tau\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\\bigr)\n       \\le\\eta d_\\tau(b)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b)<\\infty).$$ In particular the left side is exactly zero when $d_\\tau(b)=0$.*\n\n\n*The finite-coordinate form permits arbitrary relative trace accuracies together with prescribed finite norm tests. Given a finite set $F\\subset A$, finitely many pairs $(a_l,b_l)$ satisfying <a href=\"#loc:relative-rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:relative-rank\">[loc:relative-rank]</a> with finite constants $C_l$, positive accuracies $\\eta_l$, and $\\rho>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $\\theta:M_k\\to A$ whose matrix-unit order-zero defects and commutators with $F$ have norm less than $\\rho$, and whose weight satisfies $$\\label{loc:TL-coordinate}\n \\tau\\bigl(a_l^{1/2}(1-\\theta(1))a_l^{1/2}\\bigr)\n     \\le\\eta_l d_\\tau(b_l)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b_l)<\\infty)$$ for every $l$, simultaneously over all the indicated traces.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-rankgap",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed $k\\ge2$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\zeta^k:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $H$ such that, for every test $(a,\\varepsilon)$, if $$\\label{cmp:u-y}\n u^k(a)=(1-f(H))a,\n \\qquad y^k(a)=g(\\zeta^k)(e_{11})^2a,$$ there is $r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k\\in A_\\omega$ satisfying $$\\label{cmp:bounded-comparison}\n (u^k(a)-\\varepsilon)_+\n   =(r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k)^*y^k(a)r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k,\n \\qquad \\|r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k\\|^2\\le32/\\varepsilon.$$ In particular the witness bound is independent of $k$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-adaptive-comparison-witness",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,
      "quantity": "\\|r_{a,\\varepsilon}^k\\|^2",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Norm ultrapower defect-to-diagonal-corner comparison.",
        "hypotheses": [
```
