<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cut-placement** [claim](#stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement): Polar placement of positive cuts, finite ideal approximation and functional-calculus witness regularization. -> Hereditary cut placements and bounded rectangular witnesses.
- **step-comparison-convention** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-convention) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-convention): Separate zero and infinite functional values, detect ideal containment and apply the normalized functional comparison criterion. -> Equivalence of strict comparison and almost unperforation.
- **step-small-classes** [claim](#stmt-ext-lem-small) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-lem-small): Split nonzero hereditary positives and find a common nonzero class below the orthogonal family. -> Small nonzero support classes.
- **step-functional-trichotomy** [claim](#stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy): Use fullness and finite cut comparisons to propagate zero values or positive finite values. -> The functional trichotomy and a finite positive cut controller.
- **step-trace-transfer** [claim](#stmt-ext-lem-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-lem-transfer): Place a cut in a finite amplification of the chosen support and exchange the squared moduli of its column entries. -> An element with unchanged extended trace values in the small support.
- **step-integer-rounding** [claim](#stmt-ext-lem-rounding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-lem-rounding): Use the scalar staircase identity and sum adjacent spectral-rank bounds. -> A lower rank approximation with support-rank error.
- **step-full-divisibility** [claim](#stmt-ext-thm-divisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-thm-divisibility): Transfer positive cutoff increments into summably small supports, round, take an increasing supremum and compare with each target. -> A single class satisfying the full divisibility sandwich.
- **step-simple-condition-c** [claim](#stmt-ext-cor-condition-c) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-cor-condition-c): Combine full almost divisibility with comparison equivalence. -> Condition (C) for the simple exact comparison route.
- **step-unital-trace-illustration** [claim](#stmt-prop-unital-approx) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-unital-approx): Choose a uniformly small support, transfer all trace values into it and round by integer cuts. -> Uniform rank approximation from below.
- **step-canonical-condition-c** [claim](#stmt-lem-c) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-c): Transport tensor regularity through the canonical Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism. -> Condition (C) for the canonical-map route.
- **step-rank-gap-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-g) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-g): Represent every functional by an extended trace and turn the uniform rank gap into strict comparison inequalities. -> Cuntz subequivalence.
- **step-cone-placement** [claim](#stmt-lem-placement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-placement): Cut a divisible representative, place equivalent matrix corners and retain selected slots. -> Hereditary matrix cones with visibility or rank control.
- **step-support-pencil** [claim](#stmt-lem-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support): Apply quadratic-form positivity on the positive spectral subspace and pass to the closure of the compressed range. -> A support compression bound and its zero case.
- **step-bounded-diagonalization** [claim](#stmt-lem-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-diagonal): Choose decreasing ultrafilter-large finite-test sets and select the largest admissible bounded solution stage. -> An exact bounded ultrapower solution.
- **step-support-transfer** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer): Divide the finite list of squared moduli, place a shared cut in both supports and choose a plateau function. -> A contractive support transfer with norm-one visibility.
- **step-two-support-shrinking** [claim](#stmt-lem-shrinking) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-2): Assume failure of a universal shrinking factor, prepare bounded columns and word tests, and derive incompatible defect relations in a Hilbert ultraproduct. -> Existence of a universal cross-compression contraction factor preserving visibility.
- **step-finite-representation-cover** [claim](#stmt-norm-finite-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-finite-cover): Use a norm-attaining pure state of an ideal quotient to prove a strict quotient norm bound, then extract finitely many cut generators. -> A finite cut cover and support-preserving norm-one reparametrization.
- **step-pure-state-excision** [claim](#stmt-norm-excision) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-excision): Establish the pure-state kernel identity by transitivity and compress using the complementary hereditary approximate unit. -> A positive contraction fixing the cyclic vector and excising the tests.
- **step-word-prototype** [claim](#stmt-norm-prototype) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-prototype): Excise the positive target, word Gram entries and residual products, then cut the excising contraction and restrict hereditarily. -> A nonzero seed with positive compression and small Gram and residual errors.
- **step-separated-cover** [claim](#stmt-norm-separated-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-separated-cover): Reparametrize the current finite cover before each shrinking operation and successively treat all prescribed coefficient pairs. -> Separated hereditary seeds retaining representation coverage.
- **step-finite-row-cones** [claim](#stmt-norm-finite-stage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-finite-stage): Combine local models in a nearly isometric row, taper word levels and compress hereditary cones. -> Finite-stage order-zero, norm-centrality and visibility bounds.
- **step-norm-visible-central-cones** [claim](#stmt-prop-norm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-finite-stage-2): Increase finite dense test sets and word depth while decreasing finite-stage errors. -> Central ultrapower matrix cones with simultaneous coordinate representation visibility.
- **step-tracial-lipschitz** [claim](#stmt-tr-lipschitz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-lipschitz): Estimate orthogonal spectral blocks in tracial norm, approximate by finite spectral resolutions and apply the result to a selfadjoint row dilation. -> Matrix-size-independent calculus and row regularization bounds.
- **step-bounded-trace-cones** [claim](#stmt-prop-bounded-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tr-lipschitz-2): Use convex nuclear return maps, weighted support budgets, row regularization and a second commutant pencil followed by polar correction. -> Exact order-zero cones with uniform bounded-trace centrality and mass estimates.
- **step-cone-joining** [claim](#stmt-lem-join) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-join): Construct a continuous tensor-cone model using a flip path and evaluate it on commuting order-zero maps. -> A joined cone dominating both weights.
- **step-bounded-trace-filling** [claim](#stmt-loc-bounded-fill) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-bounded-fill): Pass to the uniform tracial quotient, repeatedly join fractional fillers, lift the map and select a common good coordinate. -> Uniform finite-coordinate trace filling with small tracial algebraic defects.
- **step-relative-trace-ideal** [claim](#stmt-loc-sigma-ideal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-sigma-ideal): Use polar cuts and rank calculus to establish ideality and representative independence, then diagonalize a quasicentral approximate unit with uniform rank predicates. -> A closed relative trace ideal with commuting absorbing contractions.
- **step-localized-trace-filling** [claim](#stmt-prop-localized-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-localized-trace): Localize on hereditary approximate-unit cuts, choose accuracies after finite rank multiplicities, remove the relative ideal and join reindexed maps for finite families. -> Norm-central trace fillers and arbitrary simultaneous relative finite-coordinate accuracies.
- **step-coordinate-ideal-visibility** [claim](#stmt-prop-idealvisibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-idealvisibility): Reparametrize norm-visible weights, join and diagonalize finite test families, and use primitive ideal separation followed by an extra cut. -> Exact coordinate Cuntz inequalities with finite varying ideal multiplicities.
- **step-adaptive-rank-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-rankgap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-rankgap): Freeze norm maps and multiplicities, choose reciprocal relative trace accuracies, establish exact coordinate inequalities before multiplying and regularize witnesses across a spectral margin. -> Exact ultrapower defect comparisons with matrix-size-independent witness norms.
- **step-infinite-central-slots** [claim](#stmt-prop-slots) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-slots): Restrict larger matrix cones to equivalent slots and diagonalize growing finite slot families using the fixed individual witness bounds. -> Countably many orthogonal equivalent central slots and cut-defect witnesses.
- **step-elementary-tensor-ideal** [claim](#stmt-cen-elementary-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cen-elementary-tensor): Excise first-leg coefficients while retaining their norm, then apply a joint spectral tensor cut in the ideal quotient. -> A nonzero positive elementary tensor in the ideal.
- **step-nuclear-tensor-ideals** [claim](#stmt-cen-nuclear-tensor-ideals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cen-nuclear-tensor-ideals): Separate a hypothetical missing ideal part by an irreducible representation, factor its tensor legs and use elementary tensors to establish faithful quotient representation. -> Generation of every tensor ideal by its positive elementary tensors.
- **step-simultaneous-domination** [claim](#stmt-cen-weighted-domination) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cen-weighted-domination): Represent finite functional tests cyclically, compare support-representation kernels and use positive-cone separation followed by weak-to-norm convex closure. -> A common finite compression list for all finite operator tests.
- **step-central-filler** [claim](#stmt-prop-central-filler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-central-filler): Extend dense cut comparisons to all positive elements, pack simultaneous compressions into slots, compress with approximate units and diagonalize bounded central tests. -> A bounded central element filling the matrix-cone defect after multiplication by the original algebra.
- **step-dimension-drop-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-dimensiondrop) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dimensiondrop): Generate an explicit continuous matrix model and decompose arbitrary representations of the relations into endpoint and interior summands. -> Unital prime dimension-drop homomorphisms.
- **step-prime-block-amplification** [claim](#stmt-abs-prime-block-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-abs-prime-block-map): Solve the compatible congruences and amplify each endpoint matrix leg pointwise. -> A unital map into a consecutive-size prime block.
- **step-approximate-retraction** [claim](#stmt-abs-approximate-retraction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-abs-approximate-retraction): Extend the matrix-valued factor of a nuclear approximation and compose with its return map. -> A completely positive contractive approximate retraction.
- **step-central-jiang-su-map** [claim](#stmt-prop-z-map) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-z-map): Approximate finite Jiang–Su tests through stages, compose with block maps, lift completely positively and diagonalize multiplied quotient defects. -> A unital Jiang–Su homomorphism into the central annihilator quotient.
- **step-connected-unitary-lifting** [claim](#stmt-abs-connected-unitaries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-abs-connected-unitaries): Cancel prime-block determinant winding using coprime endpoint multiplicities, pass to the inductive limit and lift finite products of selfadjoint exponentials. -> Connected Jiang–Su unitary groups and identity-component quotient unitary lifts.
- **step-finite-unitary-criterion** [claim](#stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion): Evaluate a connected half-flip through commuting central maps, lift it to actual unitaries and use multiplied quotient estimates on finite elementary-tensor approximations. -> Finite multiplier-unitary commutation and range approximations.
- **step-absorption-intertwining** [claim](#stmt-prop-absorption) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absorption): Build isometric embeddings with summable changes and retain every chosen target preimage in later domain tests. -> A surjective isometric homomorphism onto the Jiang–Su tensor product.
- **step-condition-c-absorption** [claim](#stmt-thm-condition-c) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absorption-2): Combine the central Jiang–Su homomorphism with the absorption implication. -> The central-map and absorption conclusions for the fixed free ultrafilter.
- **step-simple-absorption** [claim](#stmt-ext-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absorption-3): Use nuclear exactness to obtain condition (C) from simple comparison, then apply the common absorption theorem. -> Jiang–Su absorption in the simple comparison regime.
- **step-canonical-absorption** [claim](#stmt-cor-canonical) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absorption-4): Derive condition (C) from the canonical induced isomorphism and apply the common absorption theorem. -> Jiang–Su absorption from the canonical map.
- **step-abstract-cu-absorption** [claim](#stmt-cor-abstract-cu) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-absorption-5): Use strong self-absorption to identify the tensor algebra's canonical Cuntz map, transport intrinsic condition (C) through the abstract isomorphism and apply absorption. -> The abstract Cuntz-semigroup characterization of Jiang–Su absorption.

          "anchor": "stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A$ be simple and nonzero.           "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-ext-lem-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a simple stable $C^*$-algebra, let $a\\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $0\\ne h\\in B_+$. Suppose that $r:[0,1]\\to[0,\\infty)$ is continuous and vanishes on $[0,\\delta]$ for some $0<\\delta<1$. There is $c\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(h)_+$ such that $$\\tau(c)=\\tau(r(a))$$ for every lower-semicontinuous extended trace $\\tau$ on $B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "source_id": "PM-291.2"

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-placement",
          "exact_quote": "Assume <a href=\"#eq:pre:au\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pre:au\">[eq:pre:au]</a>–<a href=\"#eq:pre:div\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:pre:div\">[eq:pre:div]</a>.*\n\n1.  *Let $B$ be a hereditary subalgebra of a matrix algebra over $A\\otimes\\mathcal K$, let $b\\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $N\\ge1$ and $\\epsilon>0$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_N\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$ such that $$\\|\\sigma(\\psi(1))\\|=1\n     \\quad\\text{whenever}\\quad\n     \\sigma((b-\\epsilon)_+)\\ne0.$$ Its $N$ diagonal weights are orthogonal and equivalent in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_B(b)$. Each diagonal weight, cut at $1/2$, dominates a positive class $u_0$ for which $$[(b-\\epsilon)_+]\\le(N+1)u_0.$$*\n\n2.  *Let $C$ be isomorphic to a finite direct sum of hereditary subalgebras as above. For $b\\in C_+$ a contraction, $k\\ge1$, and $0<q\\le1$, there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\psi:M_k\\to\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}_C(b)$, with weight $t=\\psi(1)$, such that $$\\label{eq:pre:P}\n     d_\\sigma(t)\\le q\\,d_\\sigma(b),\\qquad\n     d_\\sigma((t-\\tfrac12)_+)\n           \\ge(q/3)d_\\sigma((b-\\tfrac12)_+)$$ for every bounded positive trace $\\sigma$ on $C$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-shrinking",
          "exact_quote": "There is a numerical constant $0<c<1$ with the following property for every $C^*$-algebra $A$ satisfying <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. If $a,b\\in A$ are positive contractions and $z\\in\\widetilde A$ satisfies $\\|p_a z p_b\\|\\le1$, there are positive contractions $a'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(a)$ and $b'\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{her}}(b)$ such that $$\\label{shr:conclusion}\n \\|p_{a'}z p_{b'}\\|\\le c$$ and, for every irreducible representation $\\sigma$ of $A$, $$\\label{shr:visibility}\n \\max\\{\\|\\sigma(a)\\|,\\|\\sigma(b)\\|\\}\\ge\\tfrac12\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\sigma(a')\\ne0\\ \\text{or}\\ \\sigma(b')\\ne0.$$ The support norms are computed in the bidual, or equivalently in the universal representation.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-norm-finite-cover",
          "exact_quote": "Let $x\\in A_+$, $\\gamma>0$, and let $(s_\\alpha)_{\\alpha\\in\\Lambda}$ be positive elements of $A$. Suppose that $\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)\\ne\\varnothing$ and that, for every $\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$, some $\\sigma(s_\\alpha)$ is nonzero. Then there are $\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_m$ and $t>0$ such that $$\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n \\max_{1\\le i\\le m}\\|\\sigma(s_{\\alpha_i})\\|>t.$$ Thus the nonvanishing loci of the cuts $(s_{\\alpha_i}-t)_+$ still cover $\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$. For a finite covering family of positive contractions, continuous functional calculus can replace its members by positive contractions with the same supports and with maximum represented norm equal to one at every $\\sigma\\in\\mathcal K(x,\\gamma)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"

          "exact_quote": "Suppose that $A$ is separable and satisfies <span class=\"upright\">(C)</span>. For every positive contraction $x\\in A$, every $\\gamma>0$, and every integer $k\\ge2$, there are c.p.c. maps $\\nu_n:M_k\\to A$ such that $$\\nu:M_k\\longrightarrow A_\\omega\\cap A',\n \\qquad \\nu(z)=[(\\nu_n(z))_n],$$ is order zero, and there is a number $\\kappa>0$ for which $$\\label{norm:coordinate-visibility}\n \\begin{gathered}\n \\text{for every }n\\in\\mathbb N\\text{ and every irreducible representation }\\sigma\n \\text{ of }A,\\\\\n \\|\\sigma(x)\\|\\ge\\gamma\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n \\|\\sigma(x\\nu_n(1))\\|\\ge\\kappa .\n \\end{gathered}$$ One may take $\\kappa=\\gamma^2/32$.           "anchor": "stmt-tr-lipschitz",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal M$ be a von Neumann algebra with a finite normal trace $T$, let $H=H^*\\in\\mathcal M$, and let $B\\in\\mathcal M$. If $f$ is real-valued and Lipschitz on an interval containing the spectrum of $H$, then $$\\|[f(H),B]\\|_{2,T}\n \\leq\\operatorname{Lip}(f)\\|[H,B]\\|_{2,T}.$$ Consequently, if $W$ is a finite row, $a=a^*$ and $m=m^*$ are compatible contractions, and $$R=W(1+W^*W)^{-1/2},$$ then $\\|R\\|\\leq1$ and $$\\|aR-Rm\\|_{2,T}\\leq\\|aW-Wm\\|_{2,T}.$$ The constants do not depend on the size of the matrices or on $\\|W\\|$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-bounded-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $k\\geq2$ and put $$L=(20k)^2,\\qquad C_k=1+4L.$$ Let $F\\subset D$ be a finite set of selfadjoint contractions, let $\\mathcal P\\subseteq F$ consist of positive contractions, and let $0<\\delta<1$. For every $\\varepsilon>0$ there is a c.p.c. order-zero map $V:M_k\\to D$ such that, for every $\\tau\\in T_{\\leq1}(D)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{\\|x\\|\\leq1}\\|[a,V(x)]\\|_{2,\\tau}\n &<\\varepsilon &&(a\\in F),\\label{tr:central-target}\\\\\n \\tau(aV(1))\n &\\geq \\frac\\delta3\\tau(a)-C_k\\delta^{3/2}-\\varepsilon\n &&(a\\in\\mathcal P).\\label{tr:mass-target}\n\\end{aligned}$$ In particular, $k$ is fixed before the accuracy is chosen; no bound on the number of nuclear approximation summands is required.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-bounded-trace-cones",

        "Tracial Lipschitz calculus"

      "output": "Exact order-zero cones with uniform bounded-trace centrality and mass estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-tr-lipschitz-2"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-join",
          "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a $C^*$-algebra, and let $\\phi_1,\\phi_2:M_k\\to B$ be c.p.c. order-zero maps with commuting ranges. Set $h_i=\\phi_i(1)$. There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to\nC^*(\\phi_1(M_k),\\phi_2(M_k))$ such that $$\\label{loc:join-weight}\n \\phi(1)=1-(1-h_1)(1-h_2)=h_1+h_2-h_1h_2.$$ Its weight dominates both $h_1$ and $h_2$. If both original maps commute with a subset of an ambient algebra, the joined map also commutes with that subset.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-loc-bounded-fill",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D\\subseteq A$ be hereditary, $k\\ge2$, $F\\subset D$ finite, and $\\mathcal P\\subset D$ a finite set of positive contractions. For every $\\rho>0$ there is a c.p.c. map $\\mu:M_k\\to D$ such that, for all matrix units $e_{ij},e_{rs}$ and all $x\\in F$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|[\\mu(e_{ij}),x]\\|_{2,T_{\\leq1}(D)}&<\\rho,\\label{loc:fill-comm}\\\\\n \\|\\mu(e_{ij})\\mu(e_{rs})-\\mu(1)\\mu(e_{ij}e_{rs})\n       \\|_{2,T_{\\leq1}(D)}&<\\rho,\\label{loc:fill-oz}\\\\\n \\sigma\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-\\mu(1))a^{1/2}\\bigr)&<\\rho\n       \\quad(a\\in\\mathcal P,\\ \\sigma\\in T_{\\leq1}(D)).\n       \\label{loc:fill-trace}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-localized-trace",
          "exact_quote": "Let $k\\ge2$, $b\\in A_+$, and let $a\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(b)$ be a positive contraction. Suppose that, for some finite $C>0$, $$\\label{loc:relative-rank}\n d_\\tau(a)\\le C d_\\tau(b)\\qquad\\text{for every }\\tau.$$ There is a c.p.c. order-zero map $\\phi:M_k\\to A_\\omega\\cap A'$ with weight $h$ such that every positive contractive representative $(h_n)$ of $h$ has the following property: for every $\\eta>0$, on a common $\\omega$-large set of coordinates, $$\\label{loc:TL}\n \\tau\\bigl(a^{1/2}(1-h_n)a^{1/2}\\bigr)\n       \\le\\eta d_\\tau(b)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b)<\\infty).$$ In particular the left side is exactly zero when $d_\\tau(b)=0$.*\n\n\n*The finite-coordinate form permits arbitrary relative trace accuracies together with prescribed finite norm tests. Given a finite set $F\\subset A$, finitely many pairs $(a_l,b_l)$ satisfying <a href=\"#loc:relative-rank\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"loc:relative-rank\">[loc:relative-rank]</a> with finite constants $C_l$, positive accuracies $\\eta_l$, and $\\rho>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $\\theta:M_k\\to A$ whose matrix-unit order-zero defects and commutators with $F$ have norm less than $\\rho$, and whose weight satisfies $$\\label{loc:TL-coordinate}\n \\tau\\bigl(a_l^{1/2}(1-\\theta(1))a_l^{1/2}\\bigr)\n     \\le\\eta_l d_\\tau(b_l)\n \\qquad(d_\\tau(b_l)<\\infty)$$ for every $l$, simultaneously over all the indicated traces.",
          "source_id": "PM-291.2"
        }
      ],

For every test $(a,\\varepsilon)$, put $$b=(a-\\varepsilon/8)_+,\\qquad\n y_n^\\nu=a^{1/2}g(h_n^\\nu)^2a^{1/2}.$$ There are integers $L_{a,\\varepsilon,n}\\ge1$ and an $\\omega$-large set $E_{a,\\varepsilon}$ on which $$\\label{cmp:I}\n [b]\\le L_{a,\\varepsilon,n}[(y_n^\\nu-\\varepsilon/20)_+]\n          \\quad\\text{in }\\mathrm{Cu}(A).$$ The integers need not be bounded as $n$ varies.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-simple-comparison-absorption**: Extended-functional strict comparison implies Jiang–Su absorption under the simple absorption theorem's separability, simplicity, nuclearity, and non-elementary hypotheses.
- **invariant-condition-c-central-map**: Condition (C) yields a unital central Jiang–Su homomorphism and tensorial absorption without a simplicity hypothesis.
- **invariant-single-class-divisibility**: A single dividing Cuntz class satisfies both sides of the full almost-divisibility sandwich; separability and nuclearity are unnecessary for this intermediate result.
- **invariant-localized-uniform-trace-defect**: Localized trace filling gives a common good coordinate set for the indicated trace family at each requested accuracy, with exact vanishing when the controlling rank is zero.
- **invariant-bounded-ultrapower-diagonalization**: Approximate satisfaction of every finite subfamily yields exact ultrapower relations when each unknown retains its own fixed norm bound.
- **invariant-central-map-implies-absorption**: Once a unital central Jiang–Su homomorphism exists, the absorption implication requires separability.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-finite-trace-only-comparison**: Test comparison only on normalized bounded traces and apply the simple absorption conclusion. -> Such tests can omit the functional inequalities recording ideal containment and the zero and infinite target cases.
- **anti-divisibility-with-different-classes**: Use different dividing classes for the lower and upper inequalities and declare condition (C). -> Separate witnesses do not establish the full almost-divisibility sandwich required by the central construction.
- **anti-coordinate-visibility-as-central-fullness**: Interpret coordinate representation visibility as fullness in the central sequence algebra. -> Visibility controls representations of the coordinate algebra and does not establish fullness in the relative commutant or annihilator quotient.
- **anti-accuracy-before-ideal-multiplicity**: Choose relative trace accuracies before fixing the coordinate ideal multiplicities. -> The available trace error need not cancel the potentially unbounded coordinate multiplicities.
- **anti-unbounded-diagonalization**: Diagonalize the raw compression coefficients or packed sums without fixed individual norm bounds. -> The resulting sequences need not represent elements of the norm ultrapower.
- **anti-quotient-error-as-unmultiplied-norm-error**: Treat a small annihilator-quotient defect as an equally small unmultiplied norm-ultrapower defect. -> An annihilator contribution disappears after multiplication by the coordinate algebra but can remain in the unmultiplied expression.
- **anti-divisibility-alone-as-absorption**: Apply the simple exact divisibility theorem as an absorption theorem without establishing the absorption hypotheses. -> The intermediate order construction does not itself supply the separable nuclear central-map construction.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The absorption construction uses a free ultrafilter, countable bounded diagonalization in a norm ultrapower, and an infinite supremum of Cuntz classes. The support-shrinking constant is established by contradiction across varying algebras. The source states: "Then the conditions can be satisfied exactly in the ultrapower." These constructions do not supply a finite executable representation of an arbitrary input algebra or a finite decision procedure for its absorption. Finite matrix checks or the arithmetic construction of individual dimension-drop block maps would verify restricted auxiliary constructions rather than the absorption conclusion.

Its proof uses weak closure and separation; it supplies no optimization objective, computable search procedure, or bound on the list size. |

A construction relating these algebraic relations to processes, communication, or a distributed execution model is ABSENT. |

This supports structural verification of operator-algebraic models whose hypotheses have already been proved; a finite executable verification procedure is ABSENT. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-simple-absorption-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The simple comparison-to-absorption conclusion requires separability, simplicity, nuclearity, non-elementarity and the specified strict comparison convention. Blocked use: ['Apply the simple absorption theorem to an elementary algebra or to an algebra whose required structural hypotheses have not been established.']
- **limit-nonsimple-condition-c** (hypothesis): The nonsimple absorption mechanism requires both almost unperforation and full almost divisibility, together with separability and nuclearity. Its unital central-map conclusion is stated for a nonzero algebra. Blocked use: ['Infer absorption from almost unperforation alone.', 'Invoke the nonzero central-map statement without establishing its hypotheses.']
- **limit-entire-functional-cone** (formal_scope): Comparison must retain the non-strict inequalities over the entire extended-functional cone and the strict inequalities at positive finite target values. Blocked use: ['Replace the comparison hypothesis by tests over normalized bounded traces alone.', 'Require strict inequalities at infinite target values or omit ideal information at zero target values.']
- **limit-common-dividing-class** (hypothesis): Full almost divisibility requires a common class satisfying both inequalities for the original class. Blocked use: ['Use different classes for the lower and upper inequalities.', 'Substitute divisibility only for compactly contained cuts without proving the full condition.']
- **limit-exact-divisibility-scope** (formal_scope): The simple exact divisibility theorem establishes an order-theoretic conclusion under simplicity, exactness, non-elementarity and strict comparison. The common absorption theorem additionally requires separability and nuclearity. Blocked use: ['Treat the exact divisibility theorem as an absorption theorem for arbitrary nonnuclear or nonseparable algebras.']
- **limit-cut-finiteness-not-global-finiteness** (edge_case): A functional finite and positive somewhere is finite on positive spectral cuts; it need not be finite on the original class. The functional trichotomy requires simplicity and a nonzero algebra. Blocked use: ['Subtract infinite functional values when estimating rounding errors.', 'Assume that finiteness somewhere implies finiteness on every class.', 'Apply the simple functional trichotomy unchanged to nonsimple algebras.']
- **limit-transfer-cut-supported-functions** (hypothesis): Trace transfer requires a simple stable algebra, a nonzero hereditary target and a continuous source function vanishing near zero. Blocked use: ['Transfer arbitrary continuous functions without the vanishing condition.', 'Use a zero hereditary target to preserve nonzero trace values.']
- **limit-rounding-support-error** (approximation): Integer spectral rounding provides a lower approximation with a support-rank error. It does not provide an exact equality between the trace and the rounded class value. Blocked use: ['Discard the support-rank error.', 'Assume a uniform bound on transferred norms or spectral-level counts from the rounding estimate.']
- **limit-divisibility-infinite-supremum** (nonconstructive): The dividing class is assembled through an increasing supremum and a norm-convergent stable block sum. No finite stopping rule for arbitrary algebraic inputs is supplied. Blocked use: ['Certify the full dividing-class conclusion from finitely many rounded increments.', 'Assign a terminating computational procedure to the full existence construction.']
- **limit-unital-normalized-trace-illustration** (hypothesis): Uniform rank approximation over tracial states is stated for a unital simple infinite-dimensional algebra with nonempty tracial state space and unnormalized matrix traces. Blocked use: ['Apply the illustration as a uniform estimate over arbitrary unbounded traces.', 'Normalize the matrix trace while retaining the same stated rank estimate.']
- **limit-spectral-margin-witness-bounds** (hypothesis): The bounded-witness construction requires subequivalence to a narrower target cut and a strictly positive separation between target thresholds. Blocked use: ['Claim the stated witness norm bound when the target thresholds coincide.', 'Use ideal membership of an uncut positive element as a uniform finite comparison bound for that entire element.']
- **limit-rank-gap-exactness** (hypothesis): The rank-gap comparison lemma requires exactness, almost unperforation and the rank inequality for every lower semicontinuous extended trace. Blocked use: ['Deduce Cuntz comparison from a finite sample of traces.', 'Apply the trace-to-functional comparison step without exactness.']
- **limit-shrinking-factor-effectivity** (nonconstructive): The support-shrinking factor is established by contradiction across varying algebras and a Hilbert ultraproduct. The source does not supply an explicit value or an effective bound on the required repetitions. Blocked use: ['Assign an explicit universal contraction factor without further evidence.', 'Derive a numerical running-time bound for support separation from the existence proof.']
- **limit-coordinate-visibility-not-central-fullness** (formal_scope): Norm visibility controls irreducible representations of the coordinate algebra. It does not assert fullness in the central relative commutant or its annihilator quotient, and it does not assume compactness of the primitive ideal space. Blocked use: ['Replace the later ideal-comparison and defect-filling constructions by an assertion of central fullness.', 'Justify the finite representation cover by an unstated compactness assumption.']
- **limit-finite-stage-errors** (approximation): The finite-stage norm construction supplies controlled order-zero and commutator errors. Exact order zero and centrality are obtained after passing to the ultrapower. Blocked use: ['Treat a finite-stage map as exactly order zero or exactly central.', 'Replace the simultaneous representation estimate by separately chosen coordinate sets for different representations.']
- **limit-bounded-trace-centrality-and-mass** (approximation): The bounded-trace cone estimate concerns tracial centrality and includes subtractive mass losses. It does not assert norm centrality or positive retained mass for every parameter choice. Blocked use: ['Use tracial centrality as norm centrality before localization and ideal removal.', 'Drop the subtractive terms from the retained-mass estimate.', 'Let the matrix size vary while treating its constants as fixed.']
- **limit-joining-commuting-ranges** (hypothesis): The joining construction assumes that the original order-zero maps have commuting ranges. Blocked use: ['Apply the stated joined-weight formula to arbitrary noncommuting matrix cones.']
- **limit-localized-relative-rank** (hypothesis): Localized norm-central trace filling requires ideal membership and a finite relative rank bound valid for every trace. The coordinate set is common to the indicated trace family, and zero controlling rank requires exact vanishing. Blocked use: ['Infer the relative trace estimate from ideal membership alone.', 'Choose different good coordinate sets for individual traces.', 'Replace exact vanishing at zero controlling rank by a small positive error.', 'Extend the relative finite-rank predicate to infinite controlling rank without a separate argument.']
- **limit-adaptive-order-of-choices** (hypothesis): Coordinate norm maps and finite ideal multiplicities must be fixed before localized trace accuracies are chosen. Exact coordinate Cuntz inequalities must be established before multiplication by varying multiplicities. Blocked use: ['Choose trace accuracy independently of the already fixed ideal multiplicities.', 'Multiply an approximate norm inequality by an unbounded coordinate multiplicity.', 'Assume the coordinate ideal multiplicities are uniformly bounded.']
- **limit-individual-bounds-for-diagonalization** (hypothesis): Every diagonalized unknown must retain a fixed individual norm bound. Comparison witnesses need not be central, and raw packed compression sums are not supplied with uniform bounds. Blocked use: ['Diagonalize unbounded compression coefficients or packed sums directly.', 'Require centrality of comparison witnesses as an additional conclusion.', 'Form norm-ultrapower elements from sequences lacking the required boundedness.']
- **limit-simultaneous-domination-existence** (nonconstructive): Simultaneous weighted domination requires a nuclear inclusion, commuting positive weights and pointwise ideal containment. Its separation argument supplies a common finite coefficient list without an effective search bound. Blocked use: ['Replace simultaneous approximation by unrelated coefficient lists for individual tests.', 'Claim an executable optimizer or a uniform coefficient-list bound from the existence statement.', 'Remove nuclearity from the stated weighted-inclusion lemma.']
- **limit-filler-equations-modulo-annihilator** (formal_scope): The central filler equation is initially an identity after multiplication by elements of the original algebra. Its unmultiplied unit relation holds after passage to the annihilator quotient. Blocked use: ['Assert the unmultiplied defect equation in the norm ultrapower for a nonunital algebra.', 'Replace the quotient unit by an assumed unit of the original algebra.']
- **limit-dimension-drop-exact-relations** (hypothesis): The dimension-drop presentation requires a unital target and exact order-zero, defect and corner relations. Maps from individual blocks are not assumed compatible. Blocked use: ['Infer an exact dimension-drop homomorphism from unchecked approximate generator relations.', 'Assemble an inductive-limit homomorphism by assuming compatibility of independently obtained block maps.']
- **limit-prime-block-and-retraction-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The block amplification requires relatively prime endpoint sizes. Approximate retraction requires a unital inclusion with a nuclear subalgebra and guarantees approximation on prescribed finite tests. Blocked use: ['Use the prime-block amplification without relative primality.', 'Treat approximate retraction as an exactly multiplicative or exactly unital retraction.']
- **limit-multiplied-quotient-errors** (formal_scope): The lifting argument controls quotient defects after multiplication by the original algebra. It does not identify the quotient norm with an unmultiplied norm-ultrapower error. Blocked use: ['Promote a small annihilator-quotient defect to an equally small unmultiplied ultrapower defect.', 'Ignore multiplication tests when proving unit and multiplicative relations in the quotient.']
- **limit-connected-unitary-lifting** (hypothesis): The stated quotient-unitary lifting assertion requires a unital quotient and a unitary in its identity component. Blocked use: ['Claim that every quotient unitary lifts without establishing the identity-component condition.']
- **limit-absorption-infinite-intertwining** (nonconstructive): The final absorption implication requires separability and a unital central Jiang–Su homomorphism. Its isomorphism is obtained as a point-norm limit, retaining earlier target preimages in subsequent tests. Blocked use: ['Certify surjectivity from finitely many unitary approximation tests.', 'Discard stored target preimages while relying on the stated dense-range argument.', 'Require nuclearity for this final implication once the central homomorphism is already established.']
- **limit-completed-cu-category** (formal_scope): The abstract criterion concerns an isomorphism in the completed Cuntz-semigroup category under separability and nuclearity. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that strict comparison in the extended-functional sense implies Jiang–Su absorption for separable simple nuclear non-elementary <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebras. This resolves the corresponding implication of the Toms–Winter regularity problem, including nonunital algebras and allowing unbounded traces. More generally, every separable nuclear <i>C</i><sup>∗</sup>-algebra whose Cuntz semigroup is almost unperforated and fully almost divisible absorbs the Jiang–Su algebra.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Comparison asks whether numerical size determines order among positive operators. Divisibility asks whether those operators can be split into pieces of nearly equal size. For nuclear $C^*$-algebras, these questions are tied to tensorial absorption of the Jiang–Su algebra $\mathcal Z$: $$A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z.$$ Jiang and Su constructed $\mathcal Z$ as a simple infinite-dimensional unital algebra with the Elliott invariant of the complex numbers <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[JS1999](#ref-JS1999)]. Its absorption is a central regularity property in the classification program. The purpose of this paper is to pass from comparison and divisibility in the Cuntz semigroup to this tensorial regularity, including algebras without a unit and algebras with ideals.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Comparison and the simple theorem

All algebras in this paper are complex. Let $\mathcal K$ be the compact operators on a separable infinite-dimensional Hilbert space. For $a,b\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, write $a\precsim b$ if $v_j^*bv_j\to a$ in norm for some sequence $(v_j)$ in $A\otimes\mathcal K$. Mutual comparison defines the Cuntz classes $[a]$. Their ordered semigroup $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ has addition given by orthogonal sum and suprema of increasing sequences <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[CEI2008](#ref-CEI2008)]. A *functional* on $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ is a map to $[0,\infty]$ preserving zero, addition, order, and increasing suprema. Write $\mathrm F(\mathrm{Cu}(A))$ for the cone of all these functionals.

<a id="ext:eq:comparison"></a>
We use the following precise extended-functional convention for *strict comparison*: for every $x,y\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$, $$\label{ext:eq:comparison}
\left.
\begin{aligned}
 \varphi(x)&\le\varphi(y)
       &&\text{for every }\varphi\in\mathrm F(\mathrm{Cu}(A)),\\
 \varphi(x)&<\varphi(y)
       &&\text{whenever }0<\varphi(y)<\infty
\end{aligned}
\right\}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\le y.$$ The second line concerns the same cone of functionals. Arithmetic at zero and infinity is extended nonnegative arithmetic; in particular, strict inequality is required precisely at positive finite target values. An algebra is *non-elementary* if it is not isomorphic to the compact operators on any Hilbert space.

<a id="stmt-ext-thm-main"></a>
<div id="ext:thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Simple comparison and absorption). *Let $A$ be a separable simple nuclear non-elementary $C^*$-algebra. If $A$ has strict comparison in the sense of Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>, then $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.*

</div>

Rørdam proved that $\mathcal Z$-absorption forces almost unperforation of the finite-matrix Cuntz semigroup and, in the simple exact unital setting, the corresponding trace comparison theorem <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Rordam2004](#ref-Rordam2004), Theorem 4.5 and Corollary 4.6]. The reverse implication is the strict-comparison-to-$\mathcal Z$-stability part of the Toms–Winter regularity problem; see <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Problems2025](#ref-Problems2025), Conjecture 5 and Problem XVIII]. Theorem <a href="#ext:thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:thm:main">1</a> gives a positive resolution of this implication with the extended-functional convention above. Its nonunital formulation includes stably projectionless algebras and allows unbounded traces.

The comparison argument has a stronger intermediate scope. Theorem <a href="#ext:thm:divisibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:thm:divisibility">11</a> proves, for every simple exact non-elementary $A$ satisfying Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>, that each $x\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and integer $N\ge1$ admit a single class $u$ with $Nu\le x\le(N+1)u$. Neither separability nor nuclearity enters that theorem. Nuclearity is used in constructing central maps from this order-theoretic information.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The nonsimple absorption mechanism

<a id="eq:pre:au"></a>
<a id="eq:pre:div"></a>
The first order property we require is *almost unperforation*: $$(l+1)x\le ly\quad\Longrightarrow\quad x\le y
 \qquad(x,y\in\mathrm{Cu}(A),\ l\ge1).
 \label{eq:pre:au}$$ The second is *full almost divisibility*: for every $x\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and integer $N\ge1$, there is $u\in\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ such that $$Nu\le x\le(N+1)u.
 \label{eq:pre:div}$$ Here $l$ is also an integer. We call the conjunction of these two properties <span class="upright">(C)</span>. The same class $u$ must satisfy both inequalities in Equation <a href="#eq:pre:div" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:div">[eq:pre:div]</a>.

The comparison convention in Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> is equivalent to almost unperforation of $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ERS2011](#ref-ERS2011), Proposition 6.2]. Lemma <a href="#lem:comparison-convention" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:comparison-convention">6</a> explains this equivalence, including the zero and infinite values. Thus the extended-functional formulation agrees with the usual Cuntz-semigroup comparison condition.

To state the central conclusion, fix a free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. The norm ultrapower and its central annihilator quotient are $$A_\omega=\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,A)/
       \{(a_n):\lim_{n\to\omega}\|a_n\|=0\},\qquad
 F_\omega(A)=(A_\omega\cap A')/\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A),$$ where $A$ is the constant copy and $\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A)=\{z\in A_\omega:zA=Az=0\}$. For separable $A$, a sequential approximate unit represents the unit of $F_\omega(A)$. The zero algebra absorbs $\mathcal Z$ immediately; the central-map statement below is formulated for nonzero algebras.

<a id="stmt-thm-condition-c"></a>
<div id="thm:condition-C" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Absorption from condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>). *Let $A$ be a nonzero separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>. For every fixed free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$, there is a unital $*$-homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. Consequently $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-canonical"></a>
<div id="cor:canonical" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (The canonical Cuntz-semigroup map). *Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra, and let $\iota:A\to A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$ be $\iota(a)=a\otimes1_\mathcal Z$. If $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota):\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z)$ is an isomorphism, then $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$.*

</div>

The isomorphism in this hypothesis is the map induced by $\iota$. Corollary <a href="#cor:canonical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:canonical">3</a> resolves positively implication (iii)$\Rightarrow$(ii) in the nonsimple Toms–Winter problem <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Problems2025](#ref-Problems2025), Section 22, Problem LXXV]. It applies to every separable nuclear $A$ with the indicated canonical map; an additional restriction on its elementary ideal subquotients is unnecessary.

Condition <span class="upright">(C)</span> depends only on the ordered-semigroup structure. Consequently, the same absorption theorem also removes the requirement that the Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism be the canonical one.

<a id="stmt-cor-abstract-cu"></a>
<div id="cor:abstract-cu" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (An abstract Cuntz-semigroup criterion). *Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra. Then $$A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \mathrm{Cu}(A)\cong\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z)
 \text{ in the category }\mathbf{Cu}.$$ The isomorphism on the right is abstract: it is not required to be induced by the first-factor embedding, or by any $*$-homomorphism.*

</div>

Here the category $\mathbf{Cu}$ retains zero, addition, order, increasing sequential suprema, and the way-below relation of the Cuntz semigroup. Corollary <a href="#cor:abstract-cu" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:abstract-cu">4</a> answers positively the abstract Cuntz-semigroup question in <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Problems2025](#ref-Problems2025), Section 9, Problem XXVI], without assuming unitality, simplicity, or stable finiteness. Separability and nuclearity remain hypotheses.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### History and the main obstacles

The comparison of positive elements and their dimension functions goes back to Cuntz and Blackadar–Handelman <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Cuntz1978](#ref-Cuntz1978), [BlackadarHandelman1982](#ref-BlackadarHandelman1982)]. The completed Cuntz semigroup of Coward–Elliott–Ivanescu <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CEI2008](#ref-CEI2008)] makes increasing suprema part of the invariant. Elliott–Robert–Santiago’s correspondence <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[ERS2011](#ref-ERS2011)] identifies its functionals with lower semicontinuous extended quasitraces. For exact algebras, Haagerup’s additivity theorem <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Haagerup2014](#ref-Haagerup2014)], in the extended form of Blanchard–Kirchberg recalled in <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ERS2011](#ref-ERS2011), Remark 4.3], makes these quasitraces traces. The associated rank is $d_\tau(a)=\lim_n\tau(a^{1/n})$: the functional corresponding to $\tau$ sends $[a]$ to $d_\tau(a)$.

The reverse comparison implication has a substantial history of partial results. Matui and Sato proved it for unital separable simple nuclear infinite-dimensional algebras with finitely many extremal traces, using excision in small central sequences and property <span class="upright">(SI) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MatuiSato2012](#ref-MatuiSato2012), Theorem 1.1]</span> . Sato, Kirchberg–Rørdam, and Toms–White–Winter extended the tracial-boundary approach to nonempty compact extreme boundaries of finite covering dimension <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Sato2012](#ref-Sato2012), Corollary 1.2], <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[KirchbergRordam2014](#ref-KirchbergRordam2014), Corollary 7.9], <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[TWW2015](#ref-TWW2015), Corollary 4.7]. Castillejos, Evington, Tikuisis, and White isolated the remaining uniform central divisibility in trace: for simple separable unital nuclear algebras with nonempty tracial state space, strict comparison together with uniform property $\Gamma$ is equivalent to $\mathcal Z$-stability <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CETW2022](#ref-CETW2022), Theorem 5.6]. Uniform property $\Gamma$ requires approximately central partitions that split the trace values of finitely many elements evenly, uniformly over all traces. The present argument constructs central maps from condition <span class="upright">(C)</span> without imposing a tracial-boundary or uniform-$\Gamma$ hypothesis.

The Toms–Winter problem also links these properties to finite nuclear dimension, a finite-colour form of completely positive approximation. Winter’s absorption theorem and the converse of Castillejos, Evington, Tikuisis, White, and Winter establish the equivalence of finite nuclear dimension and $\mathcal Z$-stability in the simple separable unital nuclear non-elementary setting <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Winter2012](#ref-Winter2012), Corollary 6.3]<a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[CETWW2021](#ref-CETWW2021), Theorem A]. The comparison implication is the remaining direction addressed here.

The distinction between comparison and divisibility is already visible in the range of possible rank functions of positive elements. Thiel proved that strict comparison yields almost divisibility and the canonical Cuntz-semigroup isomorphism for separable simple unital non-elementary algebras of stable rank one <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Thiel2020](#ref-Thiel2020), Corollary 8.12]. Theorem <a href="#ext:thm:divisibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:thm:divisibility">11</a> uses exactness without a stable-rank-one hypothesis. The fractional spectral discretization in Dadarlat–Toms <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[DadarlatToms2010](#ref-DadarlatToms2010), Theorem 5.1 and its proof] follows earlier rank-realization ideas of Brown–Perera–Toms <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BPT2008](#ref-BPT2008), Section 5]. Our first construction moves trace values into a hereditary subalgebra of small rank before taking integer spectral cuts. The resulting error is at most one support rank, regardless of the norm of the transferred element or the number of spectral levels. Proposition <a href="#prop:unital-approx" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:unital-approx">14</a> gives the unital uniform-trace version of this elementary construction.

For nonunital algebras, there may be no normalized trace space on which all errors can be bounded. The substitute is one fixed nonzero spectral cut of the element being divided. Simplicity makes its rank positive and finite for every functional that is finite and positive somewhere, even when the original element has infinite rank. We measure all rounding errors relative to this same cut and make them summable. Zero functionals and functionals infinite on every nonzero class are handled separately. This keeps the full extended trace cone available without requiring a common normalization; the precise three-way distinction is proved in Lemma <a href="#ext:lem:trichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:lem:trichotomy">8</a>.

Comparison and divisibility are also studied together through the notion of pureness. For the completed semigroup, the usual almost divisibility condition asks for $Nu\le x$ and $x'\le(N+1)u$ whenever $x'$ is compactly contained in $x$; together with almost unperforation this defines pureness <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[APT2018](#ref-APT2018)]. Full almost divisibility as used here implies that condition. For separable simple unital non-elementary algebras of locally finite nuclear dimension, Winter proved that pureness implies Jiang–Su absorption <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Winter2012](#ref-Winter2012), Corollary 6.2]. Under the same local nuclear-dimension hypothesis, he also obtained an abstract criterion using $W(A)$, the Cuntz semigroup of positive elements in finite matrices over $A$ <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Winter2012](#ref-Winter2012), Corollary 6.4]. Corollary <a href="#cor:abstract-cu" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:abstract-cu">4</a> instead uses the completed invariant $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and has no local nuclear-dimension assumption. The tensor-product approach to regularity of abstract Cuntz semigroups was developed by Antoine, Perera, and Thiel <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[APT2018](#ref-APT2018)]; more recent work of Antoine, Perera, Thiel, and Vilalta characterizes pureness by controlled comparison and functional divisibility <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[APTV2024](#ref-APTV2024), Theorem C]. Regularity of $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ and regularity of the central sequence algebra are distinct inputs. In particular, Perera, Thiel, and Vilalta characterize Jiang–Su absorption by pureness of the central sequence algebra for every separable $A$ <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[PTV2025](#ref-PTV2025), Theorem 3.6]. Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a> constructs the required central structure from the stated order properties of $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ itself.

For the central construction, a *matrix cone* means a completely positive contractive order-zero map from $M_k$: it preserves orthogonality of positive elements. Its *weight* is the image of $1_{M_k}$. Norm-central cones take values in $A_\omega\cap A'$, with coordinate maps whose centrality and order-zero errors tend to zero along $\omega$.

Robert and Tikuisis developed a nonsimple route from regularity to central structure using finite nuclear dimension <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[RobertTikuisis2017](#ref-RobertTikuisis2017), Theorem 1.1]. For separable algebras of finite nuclear dimension, they characterized absorption by the existence of two full orthogonal elements in the central sequence algebra <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[RobertTikuisis2017](#ref-RobertTikuisis2017), Theorem 1.2]. Our hypothesis is instead the order condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>, with no bound on nuclear dimension. The nonsimple problem has a different obstruction. A positive element can be large in every representation of interest without a single uniform integer controlling all the associated ideal comparisons. At the same time, traces may be finite only after hereditary localization. The proof treats these two forms of largeness separately. Pure-state compression and support shrinking construct norm-visible central matrix cones. Convex order-zero nuclear approximations, originating in the work of Hirshberg, Kirchberg, and White <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[HKW2012](#ref-HKW2012)] and refined by Brown, Carrión, and White <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[BCW2019](#ref-BCW2019)], supply cones large for bounded traces. We localize the latter estimates and then remove a relative trace ideal to obtain norm centrality.

The matrix-cone calculus uses Winter and Zacharias’s structure theorem for order-zero maps <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[WZ2009](#ref-WZ2009)]. The final packing step is an instance of Gabe’s approximate domination theorem <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Gabe2020](#ref-Gabe2020)]; its weighted-inclusion form is proved here. Choi–Effros lifting and Arveson extension allow us to retain completely positive maps during diagonalization <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[CE1976](#ref-CE1976), [Arveson1969](#ref-Arveson1969)]. The absorption step uses the structural properties of $\mathcal Z$ established by Jiang–Su, Toms–Winter, and Rørdam–Winter <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[JS1999](#ref-JS1999), [TW2007](#ref-TW2007), [RW2010](#ref-RW2010)]. Nawata’s nonunital central dimension-drop criterion <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Nawata2013](#ref-Nawata2013), Proposition 5.1] is implemented here by an explicit lifting and intertwining argument.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Proof strategy and organization

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-flow">1</a> separates the two routes into condition <span class="upright">(C)</span> from the common central-sequence argument. The full proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a> is contained in this paper. Its concrete target, for each $p\ge2$, is a matrix cone $\alpha:M_p\to F_\omega(A)$ and an element $s$ with $$s^*s=1-\alpha(1),\qquad \alpha(e_{11})s=s.$$ Thus $s$ fills the cone’s defect inside one diagonal corner. These relations give a unital map from the prime dimension-drop algebra $I(p,p+1)$, whose continuous functions on $[0,1]$ take values in $M_p\otimes M_{p+1}$ and have endpoint values in $M_p\otimes1$ and $1\otimes M_{p+1}$, respectively. Such maps supply the central copy of $\mathcal Z$.

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> introduces the common Cuntz-semigroup and trace conventions, together with the support calculus for spectral cuts. Sections <a href="#ext:sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sec:preliminaries">3</a>–<a href="#ext:sec:divisibility" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sec:divisibility">5</a> then prove the simple exact almost-divisibility theorem. Trace values are transferred to successively smaller hereditary supports, rounded at integer levels, and summed in one norm-convergent stable block sum. A fixed nonzero cut controls the summable errors even when the original class has infinite functional value. Comparison supplies the two sides of the divisibility sandwich, completing the simple route to condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>.

Section <a href="#sec:support-tools" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:support-tools">6</a> begins the common absorption argument. It derives condition <span class="upright">(C)</span> from the canonical Cuntz-semigroup map and develops its consequences for rank comparison and divisible matrix cones. It also records the ultrapower and bounded diagonalization tools used in the central constructions. Sections <a href="#shr:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="shr:section">7</a> and <a href="#norm:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:section">8</a> construct the norm-visible cones. Two-support shrinking preserves the representations detecting a prescribed positive cut while making cross terms small. Pure-state word models can then be combined into approximately central matrix cones. Their norm visibility supplies finite coordinate ideal multiplicities. Sections <a href="#sec:tracial" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tracial">9</a> and <a href="#sec:localization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:localization">10</a> construct the tracial cones. Two support compressions keep their errors independent of the number of nuclear approximation summands; hereditary localization and a relative trace ideal turn tracial centrality into norm centrality.

Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">11</a> combines these constructions in that order. Its trace accuracies depend on the already fixed ideal multiplicities. Spectral margins first produce exact coordinate Cuntz inequalities and then bound the comparison witnesses independently of matrix size. This permits a slowly growing supply of orthogonal equivalent central slots. Section <a href="#cen:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cen:section">12</a> packs finite simultaneous compressions into those slots; approximate-unit tests make the packed element bounded and central. Section <a href="#abs:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:section">13</a> turns the resulting relations into dimension-drop maps, constructs a single unital central copy of $\mathcal Z$, and performs the nonunital intertwining. Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">14</a> assembles the four stated results.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Cuntz comparison and support calculus

We collect the comparison and trace facts used by both routes into condition (C). Cut placement lets us move positive elements into prescribed hereditary subalgebras. The trace correspondence then makes numerical estimates available to the Cuntz semigroup, with zero and infinite values retained.

All tensor products are spatial unless a maximal tensor product is explicitly indicated. Unitization is denoted by a tilde. We permit the adjoined unit in formulas, but maps and constructed elements take values in the stated algebra. We write $\mathcal K$ for the compact operators on a separable infinite-dimensional Hilbert space.

For $b\in D_+$, set $$\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_D(b)=\overline{bDb},\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_D(b)=\overline{\operatorname{span}}(DbD),\qquad
 p_b=1_{(0,\infty)}(b)\in D^{**}.$$ The ambient algebra is omitted when clear. Spectral projections are also used in representations. A comparison or support relation for a matrix over $D$ is understood in the corresponding matrix amplification.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Order zero and the Cuntz semigroup

A completely positive contractive map is abbreviated c.p.c. It has *order zero* if it preserves orthogonality of positive elements. For a c.p.c. order-zero map $\theta:E\to D$ with unital domain, the order-zero structure theorem <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[WZ2009](#ref-WZ2009)] gives $$\theta(z)=h\rho(z)=\rho(z)h,\qquad
 h=\theta(1),\qquad \rho(1)=p_h,$$ where $\rho:E\to D^{**}$ is a supporting homomorphism. Its elements act as multipliers on $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(h)$. If $f:[0,1]\to[0,1]$ is continuous and $f(0)=0$, then $f(h)\rho(z)$ belongs to $D$ and defines another c.p.c. order-zero map, denoted $f(\theta)$. A *matrix cone* means a c.p.c. order-zero map from $M_k$, for some $k$.

<a id="eq:pre:cuts"></a>
<a id="ext:eq:cu-facts"></a>
For $a,b\in(D\otimes\mathcal K)_+$, the notation $a\precsim b$ means that $r_j^*br_j\to a$ in norm for suitable $r_j\in D\otimes\mathcal K$. The Cuntz semigroup $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$ consists of equivalence classes for the associated mutual comparison relation, with addition given by orthogonal sum. We use its usual sequential-supremum structure <a id="pm-cite-39"></a>[[CEI2008](#ref-CEI2008)]; in particular, $$\label{eq:pre:cuts}
 [(a-\epsilon)_+]\ll[a],
 \qquad [a]=\sup_{\epsilon>0}[(a-\epsilon)_+].$$ Here $x\ll y$ means that whenever an increasing sequence has supremum at least $y$, one of its terms is at least $x$. We will use $[a+c]\le[a]+[c]$ and the identities $$\label{ext:eq:cu-facts}
 [z^*z]=[zz^*],\qquad
 c\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b)_+\Longrightarrow[c]\le[b],\qquad
 [c]=\sup_{n\ge1}[(c-1/n)_+].$$ For mutually orthogonal positive elements, the class of the sum is the sum of the classes: the row of their square roots compares the sum with its diagonal matrix. Subequivalence to a fixed positive element is closed under norm limits in the left variable, by choosing a sufficiently good comparison witness for each approximant. The semigroup $W(D\otimes\mathcal K)$, defined using finite matrices over the stable algebra, is $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$.

We record the elementary comparison facts which will control the locations and norms of witnesses.

<a id="stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement"></a>
<div id="lem:pre:cut-placement" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Cuts, placement, and witness bounds). *Let $D$ be a $C^*$-algebra.*

1.  *If $a,b\in D_+$ and $\|a-r^*br\|<\epsilon$, then there is $v\in D$ with $$v^*v=(a-\epsilon)_+,\qquad vv^*\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b).$$ Consequently, if $a\precsim b$, the hereditary algebra of every positive cut of $a$ can be placed in $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b)$ by a polar isometry.*

2.  *If $a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}(b)_+$, then for every $\epsilon>0$ there is a finite integer $L$ such that $$[(a-\epsilon)_+]\le L[b].$$ If $[a]\le L[b]$, every positive cut of $a$ still compares to $L[(b-\delta)_+]$ for a sufficiently small $\delta>0$.*

3.  *Comparisons between elements of a hereditary subalgebra can be witnessed in that subalgebra. Comparisons between specified matrix corners can be witnessed by the appropriate rectangular matrices.*

4.  *If $u\precsim(y-\beta)_+$, where $0\le\alpha<\beta$, then for each $\eta>0$ there is $r\in D$ such that $$\|u-r^*(y-\alpha)_+r\|<\eta,\qquad
     \|r\|^2\le\frac{\|u\|+\eta}{\beta-\alpha}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-pre-cut-placement</summary>

*Proof.* For the first assertion, choose $\|a-r^*br\|<\delta<\epsilon$, and a continuous $g\ge0$, vanishing on $[0,\delta]$, which is one on $[\epsilon,\|a\|]$. For $z=b^{1/2}rg(a)$, $$(\delta-\|a-r^*br\|)\,g(a)^2
 \le z^*z
 \le (\|a\|+\|a-r^*br\|)\,g(a)^2.$$ Thus $z^*z$ and $g(a)^2$ generate the same hereditary algebra, which contains $(a-\epsilon)_+$. If $w$ is the polar isometry of $z$, conjugation by $w$ maps $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(z^*z)$ into $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(b)$. Moreover $wx\in D$ for $x\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(z^*z)$, by approximation using $w|z|=z$. Taking $v=w(a-\epsilon)_+^{1/2}$ proves the assertion. Its matrix version gives the claimed hereditary placement.

For the second assertion, approximate $a^{1/2}$ by a finite sum $q=\sum_i x_i b y_i$ so closely that $\|a-q^*q\|<\epsilon/2$. Write $q=XY$, where $X=(x_i b^{1/2})_i$ is a row and $Y=(b^{1/2}y_i)_i$ is a column. Then $q^*q\le\|X\|^2Y^*Y$, while $YY^*$ belongs to the hereditary algebra of $H=\operatorname{diag}(b,\ldots,b)$. Positive domination and equality of the classes of the two squared moduli give $$q^*q\precsim Y^*Y\sim YY^*\precsim H.$$ Choose a comparison witness with error less than $\epsilon/2$ and apply the first assertion to obtain the claimed cut comparison. Alternatively, for a fixed comparison witness against $\operatorname{diag}(b,\ldots,b)$, replacing $b$ by $(b-\delta)_+$ changes the compression by at most $\delta\|r\|^2$. This also proves the assertion about an extra target cut.

Let $C\subseteq D$ be hereditary and let $(e_i)$ be an approximate unit of $C$. Given $a,b\in C_+$ and a witness $r$, first choose an index for which the errors in $e_i b e_i$ and $e_i a e_i$ are small. Then $e_i r e_i\in C$, and its compression is as close to $a$ as desired. This proves the third assertion. The same compression in matrix algebras gives rectangular witnesses. More generally, an element whose two squared moduli belong to the specified hereditary corners lies in their rectangular block, as is seen by multiplying by approximate units on both sides.

For the last assertion choose $x$ with $\|u-x^*(y-\beta)_+x\|<\eta$, and put $$f(t)=
 \left(\frac{(t-\beta)_+}{(t-\alpha)_+}\right)^{1/2},
 \qquad r=f(y)x,$$ where the quotient is zero when its denominator vanishes. This is a continuous function vanishing near zero, and $$f(y)(y-\alpha)_+f(y)=(y-\beta)_+,\qquad
 f(y)^2\le(\beta-\alpha)^{-1}(y-\beta)_+.$$ The compression and the norm bound follow. If the original comparison is in the stabilization, compression to the given corner first gives $x\in D$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Extended traces and rank

<a id="eq:pre:trace-rank"></a>
We allow all lower semicontinuous extended traces $\tau:A_+\to[0,\infty]$, without dense finiteness. Thus $\tau$ is additive and positively homogeneous on positives, is lower semicontinuous in norm, vanishes at zero, and satisfies $\tau(x^*x)=\tau(xx^*)$. For $b\ge0$, write $$d_\tau(b)=\lim_{j\to\infty}\tau(b^{1/j}),$$ first rescaling to a contraction when necessary. The rank is unchanged by positive scalar rescaling. Traces are extended to matrices and to stabilization using the unnormalized matrix trace: on a positive infinite matrix, the extended trace is the sum of the traces of its diagonal entries. Additivity and lower semicontinuity justify these sums, and traciality follows by summing the squared moduli of the matrix entries. For a positive contraction $b$ and $s>0$, $$\label{eq:pre:trace-rank}
 \tau(b)\le d_\tau(b),\qquad
 d_\tau((b-s)_+)\le s^{-1}\tau(b).$$ These inequalities follow from functional calculus. Rank respects Cuntz comparison. For a bounded trace we use its normal extension to the bidual, so $d_\tau(b)=\tau(p_b)$.

<a id="ext:eq:trace-representation"></a>
Every such trace defines a functional $[b]\mapsto d_\tau(b)$ on $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$. Conversely, if $A$ is exact, every $\varphi\in\mathrm F(\mathrm{Cu}(A))$ has the form $$\label{ext:eq:trace-representation}
 \varphi([b])=d_\tau(b),\qquad b\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+,$$ for a lower semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on the stabilization. Elliott–Robert–Santiago’s correspondence first gives an extended quasitrace; Haagerup’s theorem, in its extended form for exact algebras, makes it additive, and stabilization preserves the trace cone <a id="pm-cite-40"></a>[[ERS2011](#ref-ERS2011), Proposition 4.2 and Remarks 4.3 and 4.5]. Neither boundedness nor dense finiteness is imposed.

Nuclear algebras are exact. Indeed, for an ideal $J\lhd D$, the quotient norm on $A\odot(D/J)$ induced by $(A\otimes_{\min}D)/(A\otimes_{\min}J)$ dominates the spatial norm and is a $C^*$-cross-norm, as seen by elementary-tensor lifts. Here $\odot$ denotes the algebraic tensor product. Uniqueness of the tensor norm for nuclear $A$ identifies this quotient with $A\otimes_{\min}(D/J)$, giving the exact tensor-product sequence.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The comparison convention

The extended-functional convention in Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> is equivalent to almost unperforation. The non-strict inequality on the whole functional cone records ideal containment; the strict inequalities at finite positive target values then determine comparison.

<a id="stmt-lem-comparison-convention"></a>
<div id="lem:comparison-convention" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *For any $C^*$-algebra $A$, strict comparison in the sense of Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> is equivalent to almost unperforation of $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-comparison-convention"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-comparison-convention</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose first that Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> holds and that $(l+1)x\le ly$. Applying a functional gives $\varphi(x)\le\varphi(y)$, with strict inequality whenever $0<\varphi(y)<\infty$. If $\varphi(y)=0$, then $\varphi(x)=0$; if $\varphi(y)=\infty$, the non-strict inequality is automatic. Thus Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a> gives $x\le y$.

Conversely, suppose $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ is almost unperforated, and let $x=[a]$ and $y=[b]$ satisfy the two functional inequalities in Equation <a href="#ext:eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:eq:comparison">[ext:eq:comparison]</a>. In $B=A\otimes\mathcal K$, set $I=\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_B(b)$. The map that is zero on $I_+$ and infinite outside $I_+$ is a lower semicontinuous extended trace: a sum of positives belongs to an ideal exactly when both summands do, $z^*z$ belongs to it exactly when $zz^*$ does, and the ideal is norm closed. Its rank functional vanishes at $y$, so the first inequality forces $a\in I$. Lemma <a href="#lem:pre:cut-placement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pre:cut-placement">5</a>(2) now gives $x\le\sup_{n\ge1}ny$. For every functional normalized by $\varphi(y)=1$, the second inequality gives $\varphi(x)<1$. The comparison criterion of <a id="pm-cite-41"></a>[[ERS2011](#ref-ERS2011), Proposition 6.2] therefore yields $x\le y$. ◻

</details>

<a id="ext:sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Simple algebras and extended traces

The next three sections prove full almost divisibility from extended-functional comparison. Two consequences of simplicity supply the error estimates: there are arbitrarily small nonzero Cuntz classes, and one fixed positive cut has finite positive value at every nonzero functional that is finite somewhere. The first provides small hereditary supports; the second measures the errors on all of them. No nuclear approximation is used.

We work in $B=A\otimes\mathcal K$ and identify $\mathrm{Cu}(B)$ with $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$. Matrix algebras over $B$ are placed in stable corners. These placements preserve Cuntz classes: if $V$ is the corresponding multiplier isometry, then $b$ and $VbV^*$ are the two squared moduli of $Vb^{1/2}$. The trace and rank conventions are those of Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a>.

<a id="stmt-ext-lem-small"></a>
<div id="ext:lem:small" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Small nonzero classes). *If $A$ is simple and non-elementary, then $B=A\otimes\mathcal K$ is simple and has no minimal projections. For every nonzero $e\in B_+$ and integer $M\ge1$, there is a nonzero $h\in B_+$ with $$M[h]\le[e].$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-lem-small"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-lem-small</summary>

*Proof.* Matrix-unit multipliers extract a nonzero matrix entry from any nonzero ideal of $B$ and put it into every matrix position. Simplicity of $A$ then implies simplicity of $B$. If $p\in B$ were minimal, $Bp$ would be a Hilbert space with inner product $x^*y=\langle x,y\rangle p$. Left multiplication is faithful by simplicity, and the elements $xy^*$ act as rank-one operators. Since $p$ generates $B$ as an ideal, they span a dense subspace of $B$, giving $B\cong\mathcal K(Bp)$. The multiplier corner $1\otimes e_{11}$ would consequently identify $A$ with compact operators on a Hilbert space, a contradiction.

There are $M$ nonzero mutually orthogonal positives $e_1,\ldots,e_M$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{her}}(e)$. Indeed, split a nonzero positive element at two distinct nonzero spectral points. If no such points exist, the element is a multiple of a projection, whose corner is not one-dimensional. A shifted nonscalar selfadjoint in that corner has two distinct positive spectral points and can be split instead. Recursion gives the required family while preserving orthogonality.

For nonzero $f,g\in B_+$, simplicity gives $fBg\ne0$. If $0\ne v\in fBg$, then $[v^*v]=[vv^*]$ is a nonzero class below both $[f]$ and $[g]$. Iterating this observation gives $h\ne0$ with $[h]\le[e_i]$ for every $i$. Therefore $M[h]\le\sum_i[e_i]\le[e]$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy"></a>
<div id="ext:lem:trichotomy" class="lemma">

**Lemma 8** (Functional trichotomy). *Let $A$ be simple and nonzero. Every functional on $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$ is either zero, infinite on every nonzero class, or positive on every nonzero class and finite on every class $[(c-\delta)_+]$ with $c\in(A\otimes\mathcal K)_+$ and $\delta>0$. The third type consists exactly of the functionals finite and positive at some class.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-lem-trichotomy</summary>

*Proof.* Every nonzero $b\in B_+$ is full. Lemma <a href="#lem:pre:cut-placement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pre:cut-placement">5</a> therefore bounds each positive cut of any $c\in B_+$ by finitely many copies of $[b]$. If $\varphi([b])=0$, preservation of increasing suprema gives $\varphi([c])=0$ for every $c$. If $0<\varphi([b])<\infty$, the same cut comparison gives finiteness on every cut, and the first observation gives positivity on every nonzero class. These alternatives exhaust all functionals. ◻

</details>

<a id="ext:eq:controller"></a>
Write $\mathrm F_{\mathrm{fin}}(A)$ for the third type in Lemma <a href="#ext:lem:trichotomy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:lem:trichotomy">8</a>. This notation does not assert finiteness on all classes. If $\|a\|=1$ and $e=(a-1/2)_+$, then $$\label{ext:eq:controller}
 0<\varphi([e])<\infty,\qquad
 \varphi([e])\le\varphi([a])
 \quad(\varphi\in\mathrm F_{\mathrm{fin}}(A)).$$

<a id="ext:sec:rounding"></a>
### Trace transfer and spectral rounding

We approximate trace values by ranks in two steps. First, a positive function of an element that vanishes near zero is transferred into a small hereditary support without changing any trace value. Integer spectral cuts then approximate that value with an error bounded by the rank of the support. The transferred element may have large norm; the error will be independent of that norm.

<a id="stmt-ext-lem-transfer"></a>
<div id="ext:lem:transfer" class="lemma">

**Lemma 9** (Trace transfer). *Let $B$ be a simple stable $C^*$-algebra, let $a\in B_+$ be a contraction, and let $0\ne h\in B_+$. Suppose that $r:[0,1]\to[0,\infty)$ is continuous and vanishes on $[0,\delta]$ for some $0<\delta<1$. There is $c\in\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_B(h)_+$ such that $$\tau(c)=\tau(r(a))$$ for every lower-semicontinuous extended trace $\tau$ on $B$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-lem-transfer"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-lem-transfer</summary>

*Proof.* Fullness of $h$ and Lemma <a href="#lem:pre:cut-placement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pre:cut-placement">5</a> give $[(a-\delta/4)_+]\le m[h]$ for some finite $m\ge1$. Apply that lemma in $M_m(B)$, with cut parameter $\epsilon=\delta/4$ and comparison error less than $\delta/4$, to the source $\operatorname{diag}((a-\delta/4)_+,0,\ldots,0)$ and target $H=\operatorname{diag}(h,\ldots,h)$. The resulting placement matrix has only its first column nonzero, because all other diagonal entries of its initial squared modulus are zero. Thus there is a column $v=(v_i)_{i=1}^m$ such that $$v^*v=(a-\delta/2)_+,\qquad
 vv^*\in M_m(\mathop{\mathrm{her}}_B(h)).$$ Define $$g(t)=\left(\frac{r(t)}{(t-\delta/2)_+}\right)^{1/2},$$ setting it equal to zero where the denominator vanishes. It is continuous and vanishes near zero because $r$ vanishes on $[0,\delta]$. For $z_i=v_i g(a)$, we have $$\sum_{i=1}^m z_i^*z_i=r(a),\qquad
