This would give $f=0$, a contradiction. The leg representations of $D/I$ and $C/L$ are faithful, so the induced tensor representation kills no nonzero positive elementary tensor. Lemma <a href="#cen:elementary-tensor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cen:elementary-tensor">39</a> applied to its kernel proves faithfulness.

The nonzero ideal $q(J)$ therefore contains a nonzero positive tensor $\bar a\otimes\bar b$. Choose positive lifts $a\in D$, $b\in C$. Since $a\otimes b\in J+\ker q$, compression by $(1-e)\otimes(1-f)$, where $e$ and $f$ run through positive contractive approximate units of $I$ and $L$, gives $$\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(a_{e}\otimes b_f,J)\longrightarrow0,
 \quad a_e=(1-e)a(1-e),\quad b_f=(1-f)b(1-f).$$ Indeed these compressions send each term of a finite sum from $I\otimes C+D\otimes L$ to zero in norm, and they preserve $J$. The quotient images of $a_e,b_f$ remain $\bar a,\bar b$. Fix $0<t<\min\{\|\bar a\|,\|\bar b\|\}$. Once the displayed distance is less than $t^2$, <a href="#cen:product-cut" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:product-cut">[cen:product-cut]</a> yields $(a_e-t)_+\otimes(b_f-t)_+\in J_0$ with nonzero image under $q$. Faithfulness of the induced representation contradicts $\pi(J_0)=0$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-31"></a>
#### The simultaneous approximation

<a id="cen:domination-equation"></a>
Gabe’s approximate domination theorem <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[Gabe2020](#ref-Gabe2020), Definition 3.1 and Theorem 3.3] applies to an exact $C^*$-algebra $D$, an arbitrary $C^*$-algebra $E$, and nuclear completely positive maps $\Psi,\Phi:D\to E$, with $\Psi$ of order zero. It identifies the condition $\Phi(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(a))$ for every $a\in D_+$ with the following finite-set approximation: for every finite $F\subset D$ and $\delta>0$, there are $N\in\mathbb N$ and $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in E$ such that $$\label{cen:domination-equation}
 \left\|\Phi(a)-\sum_{j=1}^Nv_j^*\Psi(a)v_j\right\|<\delta
\qquad(a\in F).$$ Nuclearity is required of the maps; no nuclearity or separability assumption is imposed on $E$. We prove the weighted-inclusion case used here, so that the passage from ideal containment to one common finite list of compression coefficients is explicit.

<a id="stmt-cen-weighted-domination"></a>
<div id="cen:weighted-domination" class="lemma">

**Lemma 41** (Simultaneous domination for weighted inclusions). *Let $D\subseteq E$ be a nuclear $C^*$-subalgebra of a $C^*$-algebra $E$, and let $v,w$ be positive contractions in $\widetilde E\cap D'$. Put $\Phi(a)=va$ and $\Psi(a)=wa$ for $a\in D$; these products belong to $E$. Suppose that $$\Phi(a)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(a))\qquad(a\in D_+).$$ Then for every finite set $F\subset D$ and every $\delta>0$, there are an integer $N\ge1$ and $v_1,\ldots,v_N\in E$ such that $$\left\|va-\sum_{j=1}^N v_j^*wa v_j\right\|<\delta
 \qquad(a\in F).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cen-weighted-domination"><summary>Proof of stmt-cen-weighted-domination</summary>

*Proof.* Let $\mathcal Q$ be the cone of maps $a\mapsto\sum_{j=1}^N b_j^*wa b_j$, where $b_j\in E$. It is convex: sums concatenate the lists, and multiplication by a nonnegative scalar is absorbed into the $b_j$. It suffices first to place $\Phi$ in its point-weak closure.

<a id="cen:common-functional-model"></a>
Fix $a_1,\ldots,a_m\in D$ and $\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_m\in E^*$. We may represent all these functionals in one nondegenerate cyclic representation $\sigma:E\to\mathcal B(H)$, with cyclic vector $\xi$, in the form $$\label{cen:common-functional-model}
 \ell_i(b)=\langle\sigma(b)c_i\xi,\xi\rangle,
 \qquad c_i\in\sigma(E)'.$$ To verify this, decompose the real and imaginary parts of each $\ell_i$ into differences of positive functionals. A positive multiple of their sum dominates all the positive summands. In its GNS representation a dominated positive functional gives a bounded positive form on vectors $\sigma(b)\xi$; its representing operator commutes with $\sigma(E)$, as is seen by moving $\sigma(x)$ between the two arguments of the form. Linear combinations give the $c_i$ in <a href="#cen:common-functional-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:common-functional-model">[cen:common-functional-model]</a>. Extend $\sigma$ unitally to $E^{\sim}$ when necessary.

Put $C=C^*(1,c_1,\ldots,c_m)\subset\sigma(E)'$. The commuting representations define $$\pi:D\otimes C\longrightarrow\mathcal B(H),\qquad
 \pi(a\otimes c)=\sigma(a)c;$$ continuity for the spatial tensor norm follows from nuclearity of $D$. For $q=v,w$, let $p_q$ be the support projection of $\sigma(q)$ and let $\pi_q$ be the restriction to $p_qH$. This is a reducing subspace for $\pi$: each $q$ commutes with $D$, and each element of $C$ commutes with $E^{\sim}$.

<a id="cen:support-kernel"></a>
We claim $$\label{cen:support-kernel}
 \ker\pi_w\subset\ker\pi_v.$$ Suppose $a\otimes c\in\ker\pi_w$. Commutation with the weight and positivity give $$\sigma(wa^*a)c^*c=0.$$ Since $c^*c$ commutes with all of $\sigma(E)$, this identity persists after multiplying $\sigma(wa^*a)$ on either side by elements of $\sigma(E)$ and taking closed linear spans. The ideal hypothesis $va^*a\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(wa^*a)$ therefore gives $\sigma(va^*a)c^*c=0$. This says $\pi(a\otimes c)\sigma(v)^{1/2}=0$, and, by density of the range of $\sigma(v)^{1/2}$ in $p_vH$, gives $a\otimes c\in\ker\pi_v$. Lemma <a href="#cen:nuclear-tensor-ideals" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cen:nuclear-tensor-ideals">40</a> says that the elementary tensors in $\ker\pi_w$ generate that kernel, and proves <a href="#cen:support-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:support-kernel">[cen:support-kernel]</a>.

Let $K=\ker\pi_w$. The vectors $$\eta_b=\sigma(w)^{1/2}\sigma(b)\xi,\qquad b\in E,$$ are dense in $p_wH$: cyclicity first gives density before application of $\sigma(w)^{1/2}$, and its range is dense in its support space. Finite sums of their vector functionals have weak-\* closure equal to the positive functional cone of $(D\otimes C)/K$. Here is the separation argument. A selfadjoint element of this quotient that is nonnegative on all these functionals has a positive representing operator on $p_wH$, by the density of the $\eta_b$. Faithfulness of the quotient representation makes the element positive. Thus the polar of the cone of these vector functionals is exactly the positive cone of the quotient. The bipolar form of Hahn–Banach separation identifies its weak-\* closure with all positive functionals on that quotient. This argument also applies to a nonunital quotient.

The positive functional $$\gamma_v(x)=
 \langle\pi(x)\sigma(v)^{1/2}\xi,
                   \sigma(v)^{1/2}\xi\rangle$$ factors through this quotient by <a href="#cen:support-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:support-kernel">[cen:support-kernel]</a>. Hence, simultaneously on the finitely many tensors $a_i\otimes c_i$, it is approximated by finite sums of vector functionals for $\eta_{b_j}$. On these tensors the identities are $$\begin{aligned}
 \gamma_v(a_i\otimes c_i)&=\ell_i(va_i),\\
 \sum_j\langle\pi(a_i\otimes c_i)\eta_{b_j},\eta_{b_j}\rangle
   &=\ell_i\left(\sum_jb_j^*wa_i b_j\right).
\end{aligned}$$ They prove the required point-weak closure, with the same finite list of coefficients for all the tests. Finally, for a fixed finite list $a_1,\ldots,a_m$, the set $$\{(T(a_1),\ldots,T(a_m)):T\in\mathcal Q\}\subset E^m$$ is convex. Its weak and norm closures coincide by Hahn–Banach separation. This proves <a href="#cen:domination-equation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:domination-equation">[cen:domination-equation]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### From slots to a bounded central element

<a id="stmt-prop-central-filler"></a>
<div id="prop:central-filler" class="proposition">

<a id="cen:filler-relations"></a>
**Proposition 42**. *Let $A$ be a separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>. For each $p\ge2$ there are a c.p.c. order-zero map $\alpha:M_p\to A_\omega\cap A'$, with $h=\alpha(1)$, and an element $S\in A_\omega\cap A'$ such that $$\label{cen:filler-relations}
 \alpha(e_{11})S=S,\qquad S^*S a=(1-h)a\quad(a\in A).$$ One can choose $\|S\|\le2$. In the second equation the expression $(1-h)a$ is computed in the unitization of $A_\omega$ and belongs to $A_\omega$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-central-filler"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-central-filler</summary>

*Proof.* Fix the map and slots from Proposition <a href="#prop:slots" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:slots">38</a>. Thus $P=s_1\ge0$, all $s_j$ are central contractions, <a id="cen:slot-recall"></a>$$\label{cen:slot-recall}
 s_i^*s_j=\delta_{ij}P^2,\qquad
 \alpha(e_{11})s_j=s_j,$$ and, for every element $a$ of the countable dense family $\mathcal A$ of positive contractions fixed in Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">11</a>, closed under positive rational cuts, and every rational $\varepsilon>0$, there is a bounded element $r_{a,\varepsilon}$ with $$((1-h)a-\varepsilon)_+=r_{a,\varepsilon}^*(P^2a)r_{a,\varepsilon}.$$ Set $E=A_\omega$, $v=1-h$, $w=P^2$, and define $\Phi(a)=va$, $\Psi(a)=wa$. Both maps are completely positive and contractive, and $\Psi$ has order zero. Since $A$ is nuclear, composing finite-dimensional completely positive approximations to $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_A$ with either map proves that both maps are nuclear.

Letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ in the last comparison gives $\Phi(d)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(d))$ for $d\in\mathcal A$. We verify the extension to an arbitrary positive contraction $a\in A$. Choose $d_n\in\mathcal A$ and positive rational $\delta_n\downarrow0$ with $\|d_n-a\|<\delta_n$. The cuts $b_n=(d_n-\delta_n)_+$ belong to $\mathcal A\cap\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(a)$, by positive-element perturbation, and $b_n\to a$ in norm. Centrality of $w$ implies <a id="cen:ideal-extension"></a>$$\label{cen:ideal-extension}
 w\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_A(a)\subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(wa):
 \quad w(xay)=x(wa)y\quad(x,y\in A).$$ The same assertion follows for the closed ideal by approximation with finite sums. Consequently $\Psi(b_n)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(a))$, and $$\Phi(b_n)\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(b_n))
             \subset\mathop{\mathrm{Ideal}}_E(\Psi(a)).$$ Taking the norm limit proves the ideal hypothesis for $a$; scaling proves it for every positive element.

<a id="cen:packed-sum"></a>
Apply Lemma <a href="#cen:weighted-domination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cen:weighted-domination">41</a>. Any resulting sum packs into a single element $z=\sum_{j=1}^N s_jv_j\in E$, because, for $a\in A$, $$\label{cen:packed-sum}
 z^*az=\sum_{i,j}v_i^*s_i^*as_jv_j
      =\sum_jv_j^*(P^2a)v_j.$$ There is no uniform bound here on $z$ or on the individual $v_j$, and the coefficients need not commute with $A$. A compression by one approximate-unit element will supply both the bound needed for diagonalization and, through simultaneous tests, control of the commutators.

<a id="cen:bounded-compression"></a>
<a id="cen:first-test"></a>
<a id="cen:second-test"></a>
<a id="cen:commutator-bound"></a>
Fix a finite set $F$ of contractions in $A$, and let $\eta,\delta>0$. Choose a positive contraction $t\in A$ from an approximate unit so that, for all $a\in F$, $$\|t^2a-a\|<\eta,\qquad
 \|tat-a\|<\eta,\qquad
 \|ta^*at-a^*a\|<\eta.$$ Approximate $\Phi$ to within $\delta$, using one sum in <a href="#cen:packed-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:packed-sum">[cen:packed-sum]</a>, on $$\{t^2\}\cup\{tat,ta^*at:a\in F\}.$$ Put $S'=tz$. The $t^2$ test gives $$\label{cen:bounded-compression}
 \|S'\|^2=\|z^*t^2z\|\le\|vt^2\|+\delta\le1+\delta.$$ Also $\alpha(e_{11})S'=S'$ exactly, since $\alpha(e_{11})$ commutes with $t$ and fixes all the $s_j$. Writing $\kappa=\delta+\eta$, the three tests give <a id="cen:third-test"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \|S'^*S'a-va\|&<\kappa,\label{cen:first-test}\\
 \|S'^*aS'-va\|&<\kappa,\label{cen:second-test}\\
 \|S'^*a^*aS'-va^*a\|&<\kappa.\label{cen:third-test}
\end{aligned}$$ For the first line, multiply $\|S'^*S'-vt^2\|<\delta$ by $a$; the other two lines follow directly from their respective tests. Taking the adjoint of <a href="#cen:second-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:second-test">[cen:second-test]</a> controls $S'^*a^*S'$ as well. Now expand the full commutator square: $$\begin{aligned}
 (aS'-S'a)^*(aS'-S'a)
   ={}&S'^*a^*aS'-S'^*a^*S'a\\
     &-a^*S'^*aS'+a^*S'^*S'a.
\end{aligned}$$ Its four terms are within $\kappa$ of, respectively, $va^*a$, $-va^*a$, $-va^*a$, and $va^*a$, because $v$ commutes with $A$. Therefore $$\label{cen:commutator-bound}
 \|[a,S']\|\le2\sqrt\kappa\qquad(a\in F).$$

Take increasing finite sets dense in the unit ball of $A$ and choose $\eta,\delta\downarrow0$, always with $\delta<1$. Each resulting $S'$ has norm at most $\sqrt2$ by <a href="#cen:bounded-compression" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:bounded-compression">[cen:bounded-compression]</a>, and may be represented by a sequence bounded by $2$. Fix representatives of the finitely many matrix entries of $\alpha$ throughout. The countable diagonalization of Lemma <a href="#lem:diagonal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:diagonal">19</a>, applied to these fixed representatives and the norm tests <a href="#cen:first-test" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:first-test">[cen:first-test]</a>, <a href="#cen:commutator-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:commutator-bound">[cen:commutator-bound]</a>, and $\alpha(e_{11})S'=S'$, gives a single element $S$ of norm at most $2$. It commutes with every element of $A$, retains the fixed support equation, and satisfies <a href="#cen:filler-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:filler-relations">[cen:filler-relations]</a> on a dense set, hence on all of $A$ by continuity. Only the uniformly bounded $S'$ are diagonalized at this last step; the compression coefficients and their unbounded sums are not retained as variables. ◻

</details>

<a id="abs:section"></a>
### Dimension-drop relations and Jiang–Su absorption

Proposition <a href="#prop:central-filler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:central-filler">42</a> supplies a matrix cone and an element filling its defect. We first turn their relations into unital maps from individual prime dimension-drop algebras. These maps need not be compatible: nuclear approximation and bounded completely positive lifts will instead produce a single unital map $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. Finally, half-flip unitaries and an approximate intertwining give $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$.

This route from central dimension-drop maps to absorption is established. For separable, possibly nonunital algebras, Nawata <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[Nawata2013](#ref-Nawata2013), Proposition 5.1] states the criterion using the sequence quotient. It fits the strongly self-absorbing framework of Toms–Winter <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[TW2007](#ref-TW2007)]; a formulation for arbitrary strongly self-absorbing algebras is given in <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[PTV2025](#ref-PTV2025), Theorem 2.2]. We give the lifting and intertwining steps for the fixed ultrafilter quotient used here.

<a id="pm-h-34"></a>
#### The unit in the annihilator quotient

Put $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A)=\{x\in A_\omega:xa=ax=0\text{ for every }a\in A\},
 \qquad
 F_\omega(A)=(A_\omega\cap A')/\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A).$$ The annihilator is a closed two-sided ideal of $A_\omega\cap A'$. It is contained in that relative commutant, since all its products with $A$ vanish. If $(e_n)$ is a positive contractive sequential approximate unit of $A$, then $e=[(e_n)]\in A_\omega\cap A'$, and its class is the unit of $F_\omega(A)$. Indeed $ea=ae=a$ for $a\in A$, while for $x\in A_\omega\cap A'$ the differences $ex-x$ and $xe-x$ annihilate $A$ on both sides. For example $(ex-x)a=(ea-a)x=0$, and the other products follow in the same way.

<a id="abs:quotient-relations"></a>
Apply Proposition <a href="#prop:central-filler" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:central-filler">42</a>. The selfadjoint element $S^*S+h-e$ annihilates $A$ on the right by <a href="#cen:filler-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="cen:filler-relations">[cen:filler-relations]</a>, and therefore on the left by taking adjoints. Writing $s$ for the quotient image of $S$, and using the same letters $\alpha,h$ for the quotient images of the map and its weight, gives the exact relations $$\label{abs:quotient-relations}
 s^*s=1-h,\qquad \alpha(e_{11})s=s,
 \qquad h=\alpha(1).$$ In particular $s$ is a contraction in the quotient. The argument uses the quotient unit represented by $(e_n)$, without requiring a unit in $A$.

<a id="pm-h-35"></a>
#### An explicit dimension-drop model

For positive integers $r,s$, write $$I(r,s)=\{f\in C([0,1],M_r\otimes M_s):
 f(0)\in M_r\otimes1_s,\ f(1)\in1_r\otimes M_s\}.$$

Relations <a href="#abs:quotient-relations" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:quotient-relations">[abs:quotient-relations]</a> are the order-zero form of the dimension-drop presentation in <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[Sato2010](#ref-Sato2010), Proposition 2.1], following <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[RW2010](#ref-RW2010), Proposition 5.1, (iii)(iv)]. We give an explicit model and verify that every representation of the relations factors through it.

<a id="stmt-prop-dimensiondrop"></a>
<div id="prop:dimensiondrop" class="proposition">

**Proposition 43**. *Let $B$ be a unital $C^*$-algebra, let $p\ge2$, and suppose that a c.p.c. order-zero map $\alpha:M_p\to B$ and $s\in B$ satisfy $$h=\alpha(1),\qquad s^*s=1-h,\qquad \alpha(e_{11})s=s.$$ There is a unital homomorphism $I(p,p+1)\to B$ whose range contains $\alpha(M_p)$ and $s$. Consequently $F_\omega(A)$ receives a unital homomorphism from $I(p,p+1)$ for every $p\ge2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-dimensiondrop"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-dimensiondrop</summary>

<a id="abs:model-map"></a>
<a id="abs:model-element"></a>
<a id="abs:scalar-identity"></a>
*Proof.* *The model and its generators.* Decompose the first matrix leg of $M_{p+1}\otimes M_p$ into blocks of sizes $p$ and $1$, and put $$\begin{aligned}
 A_a(t)&=\begin{pmatrix}a\otimes1_p&0\\0&ta\end{pmatrix},
       &&a\in M_p,\label{abs:model-map}\\
 T(t)&=\sqrt{1-t}\ e_{1,p+1}^{(p+1)}\otimes1_p.
       \label{abs:model-element}
\end{aligned}$$ The upper diagonal block has size $p^2$ and the lower one size $p$. At each $t$ the map $a\mapsto A_a(t)$ is completely positive, contractive, and order zero. Direct matrix multiplication gives <a id="abs:model-relations"></a>$$\label{abs:model-relations}
 T^*T=1-A_1,\qquad A_{e_{11}}T=T,$$ and $$\label{abs:scalar-identity}
 T^*T+\sum_{i=1}^p A_{e_{i1}}TT^*A_{e_{1i}}
           =(1-t)1_{p(p+1)}.$$ For the last identity, $T^*T$ is $(1-t)$ times the bottom block projection, and the $i$th summand is $(1-t)$ times the $i$th top block projection.

Let $$j(a)=\operatorname{diag}(a\otimes1_p,a)$$ and define the boundary algebra $$B_p=\{f\in C([0,1],M_{p+1}\otimes M_p):
 f(0)\in M_{p+1}\otimes1_p,\ f(1)\in j(M_p)\}.$$ We claim that $A_a$ and $T$ generate $B_p$. First $A_1+T^*T=1$, and <a href="#abs:scalar-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:scalar-identity">[abs:scalar-identity]</a> gives the coordinate scalar function $t1$. Thus their generated algebra contains $C([0,1])1$. At $t=1$ the generators have image $j(M_p)$. At $0\le t<1$, the nonzero link $T(t)$, its adjoint, and the top matrix action yield all matrix units of $M_{p+1}\otimes1_p$: the links $A_{e_{i1}}(t)T(t)$ give the $i$th top-to-bottom units, and their products give all top-to-top units and the bottom projection. At $t=0$ these are exactly the prescribed fiber. When $0<t<1$, compression of $A_a(t)$ to the bottom block and division by the scalar $t$ gives the full bottom copy of $M_p$. Multiplying by the preceding matrix units gives all of $M_{p+1}\otimes M_p$ in that fiber.

Fiber generation and the scalar functions prove the claim in norm. More explicitly, for $f\in B_p$ and $\varepsilon>0$, at each $t$ choose an element $g_t$ of the generated algebra whose value at $t$ agrees with $f(t)$, or is within $\varepsilon/2$ of it. Continuity gives an open neighborhood on which $\|g_t-f\|<\varepsilon$. A finite subcover and a subordinate scalar partition of unity produce an element of the generated algebra within $\varepsilon$ of $f$ on the whole interval.

The representation $j$ of $M_p$ has multiplicity $p+1$, so some unitary $V\in M_{p+1}\otimes M_p$ conjugates $j(M_p)$ onto $1_{p+1}\otimes M_p$. The finite-dimensional unitary group is path connected; choose a unitary path $V(t)$ from $1$ to $V$. Conjugation by this path changes only the endpoint presentation: the boundary algebra becomes the one with fibers $M_{p+1}\otimes1_p$ at $0$ and $1_{p+1}\otimes M_p$ at $1$. Reversing the interval and interchanging the tensor legs identifies it with $I(p,p+1)$.

*Arbitrary representations of the relations.* We have identified the algebra generated by the model. To obtain the asserted map into $B$, we now show that every pair $(\alpha,s)$ in the statement gives a representation of that algebra. Represent $B$ faithfully and unitally on a Hilbert space $H$. The order-zero structure theorem gives a supporting representation $\rho:M_p\to\mathcal B(H)$, with unit the support projection of $h=\alpha(1)$, such that $\alpha(a)=h\rho(a)=\rho(a)h$. In the polar decomposition $$s=u(1-h)^{1/2},$$ the initial space of $u$ is $H_s=1_{[0,1)}(h)H$. The equation $\alpha(e_{11})s=s$ implies that $\alpha(e_{11})$ is the identity on the closed range of $s$. Since $0\le\alpha(e_{11})\le h\le1$, both $h$ and $\rho(e_{11})$ are the identity on this range. Thus $u$ is an isometry from $H_s$ into $1_{\{1\}}(h)\rho(e_{11})H$.

For $1\le i\le p$ the subspaces $\rho(e_{i1})uH_s$ are mutually orthogonal. Their direct sum $H_t$ reduces $\rho$ and is identified with $\mathbb C^p\otimes H_s$ by the unitary sending $e_i\otimes\zeta$ to $\rho(e_{i1})u\zeta$. On $H_t$ the weight is $1$ and the representation is $a\otimes1_{H_s}$. Since $H_t$ is contained in the $h=1$ space, it is orthogonal to $H_s$. The orthogonal complement $$H_r=1_{\{1\}}(h)H\ominus H_t$$ reduces both generators: $s$ and $s^*$ vanish there, and $\alpha$ acts there as the unital matrix representation $\rho|_{H_r}$. This summand is therefore obtained by evaluating $B_p$ at $1$.

<a id="abs:arbitrary-representation"></a>
On $H_t\oplus H_s\cong\mathbb C^{p+1}\otimes H_s$ the generators are precisely $$\label{abs:arbitrary-representation}
 \alpha(a)=
 \begin{pmatrix}a\otimes1_{H_s}&0\\0&h_s\rho_s(a)\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 s=e_{1,p+1}^{(p+1)}\otimes(1-h_s)^{1/2},$$ where $h_s$ and $\rho_s$ denote the source restrictions, with $\rho_s=0$ on $\ker h_s$. Split $H_s$ as $H_0\oplus H_{\mathrm{mid}}$ according to the spectral sets $\{0\}$ and $(0,1)$. On $\mathbb C^{p+1}\otimes H_0$, <a href="#abs:arbitrary-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:arbitrary-representation">[abs:arbitrary-representation]</a> is the amplification of evaluation at $0$: its bottom matrix action is zero and its link has coefficient $1$. On $H_{\mathrm{mid}}$ the representation $\rho_s$ is unital and commutes with $h_s$. Continuous functional calculus therefore gives a unital representation $$C([0,1])\otimes M_p\longrightarrow\mathcal B(H_{\mathrm{mid}}),
 \qquad f\otimes a\longmapsto f(h_s)\rho_s(a).$$ Amplifying by $M_{p+1}$ and restricting to $B_p$ sends <a href="#abs:model-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:model-map">[abs:model-map]</a>–<a href="#abs:model-element" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:model-element">[abs:model-element]</a> to <a href="#abs:arbitrary-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:arbitrary-representation">[abs:arbitrary-representation]</a> on this summand. Together with the two endpoint representations this gives a unital representation of $B_p$ on all of $H$, sending its generators to $\alpha(a)$ and $s$. Since these generators generate $B_p$, the image lies in the represented copy of $B$. The identification of $B_p$ with $I(p,p+1)$ proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-36"></a>
#### From prime blocks to a central copy of $\mathcal Z$

We use the standard structural properties of the Jiang–Su algebra. It is separable, unital, and nuclear, and is an injective unital inductive limit of prime dimension-drop blocks <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[JS1999](#ref-JS1999), Proposition 2.5 and its proof]. It is also strongly self-absorbing <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[JS1999](#ref-JS1999), Theorems 7.6 and 8.7]; see the framework in <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[TW2007](#ref-TW2007)] and the later construction in <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[RW2010](#ref-RW2010)]. We next give the passage from the individual block maps to a homomorphism of the limit, since the block maps obtained above need not be compatible.

<a id="stmt-abs-prime-block-map"></a>
<div id="abs:prime-block-map" class="lemma">

**Lemma 44**. *If $r$ and $s$ are relatively prime, there is a unital homomorphism $I(r,s)\to I(p,p+1)$ for some $p\ge2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-abs-prime-block-map"><summary>Proof of stmt-abs-prime-block-map</summary>

*Proof.* The congruences $p\equiv0\pmod r$ and $p\equiv-1\pmod s$ have a simultaneous solution, and adding a sufficiently large multiple of $rs$ makes it at least $2$. Write $p=mr$, $p+1=ns$, and use the unital amplifications $$M_r\longrightarrow M_p,\quad a\longmapsto a\otimes1_m,
 \qquad
 M_s\longrightarrow M_{p+1},\quad b\longmapsto b\otimes1_n.$$ Their tensor product, applied pointwise, sends the $0$ boundary $M_r\otimes1_s$ into $M_p\otimes1_{p+1}$ and the $1$ boundary $1_r\otimes M_s$ into $1_p\otimes M_{p+1}$. ◻

</details>

The next lemma lets us approximately retract a finite subset of $\mathcal Z$ into a sufficiently late stage. Composing the retraction with any homomorphism from that stage gives a completely positive map defined on all of $\mathcal Z$. Approximation on the prescribed elements and their products makes this map approximately multiplicative.

<a id="stmt-abs-approximate-retraction"></a>
<div id="abs:approximate-retraction" class="lemma">

**Lemma 45**. *Let $D\subset L$ be a unital inclusion with $D$ nuclear. Given a finite subset $G\subset D$ and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a c.p.c. map $R:L\to D$ with $\|R(d)-d\|<\varepsilon$ for $d\in G$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-abs-approximate-retraction"><summary>Proof of stmt-abs-approximate-retraction</summary>

*Proof.* Choose a nuclear approximation $D\xrightarrow{\theta}M_k\xrightarrow{\eta}D$, with both maps completely positive and contractive, whose composition is within $\varepsilon$ of the identity on $G$. The matrix-valued extension theorem of Arveson <a id="pm-cite-65"></a>[[Arveson1969](#ref-Arveson1969), Theorem 1.2.3] extends $\theta$ to a c.p.c. map $\widetilde\theta:L\to M_k$. Then $R=\eta\widetilde\theta$ has the stated properties. Applying the approximation also to $1_D$ makes $R(1_L)$ as close to $1_D$ as desired; an exactly unital retraction is unnecessary. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-z-map"></a>
<div id="prop:Z-map" class="proposition">

**Proposition 46**. *Let $A$ be a nonzero separable nuclear $C^*$-algebra satisfying <span class="upright">(C)</span>, and fix any free ultrafilter $\omega$ on $\mathbb N$. Then there is a unital homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-z-map"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-z-map</summary>

*Proof.* Realize $\mathcal Z$ as the closure of an increasing sequence of its injectively included prime dimension-drop stages $D_l$. By Proposition <a href="#prop:dimensiondrop" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:dimensiondrop">43</a> and Lemma <a href="#abs:prime-block-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:prime-block-map">44</a>, every $D_l$ has a unital homomorphism $\chi_l:D_l\to F_\omega(A)$.

<a id="abs:quotient-approximation"></a>
Let $\mathcal Z_0$ be a countable dense $*$-subalgebra over $\mathbb Q+i\mathbb Q$ containing $1$, and exhaust it by finite sets. For any finite set of contractions $F\subset\mathcal Z_0$ and any $\varepsilon>0$, approximate $F\cup F^2\cup\{1\}$ by one stage, and apply Lemma <a href="#abs:approximate-retraction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:approximate-retraction">45</a> to those stage approximants. With the errors chosen sufficiently small, the resulting c.p.c. map $R:\mathcal Z\to D_l$, viewed in $\mathcal Z$, satisfies $$\|R(x)-x\|<\varepsilon
 \quad(x\in F\cup F^2\cup\{1\}).$$ Consequently, for $x,y\in F$, $$\|R(xy)-R(x)R(y)\|
 \le\|R(xy)-xy\|+\|xy-R(x)R(y)\|<3\varepsilon.$$ Here the norm calculation takes place in $\mathcal Z$, with $D_l$ included isometrically. Composing with $\chi_l$ produces c.p.c. maps $\beta_n:\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$ such that $$\label{abs:quotient-approximation}
 \|\beta_n(xy)-\beta_n(x)\beta_n(y)\|\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \|\beta_n(1)-1\|\longrightarrow0$$ for every $x,y\in\mathcal Z_0$. Including successively larger scaled tests handles elements outside the unit ball.

We now turn these approximate quotient relations into exact ones. Put $C_A=A_\omega\cap A'$, and let $q:C_A\to F_\omega(A)$ be the quotient map. By the nuclear completely positive lifting theorem of Choi–Effros <a id="pm-cite-66"></a>[[CE1976](#ref-CE1976), Theorem 3.10 and Corollary 3.11], each $\beta_n$ has a c.p.c.  lift $L_n:\mathcal Z\to C_A$. Regarding $L_n$ as a map into $A_\omega$ and applying the same theorem to $\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,A)\to A_\omega$ gives a c.p.c. lift $$\widetilde L_n:\mathcal Z\longrightarrow\ell^\infty(\mathbb N,A),
 \qquad \widetilde L_n(x)=(L_{n,k}(x))_{k\ge1}.$$ Each coordinate map $L_{n,k}:\mathcal Z\to A$ is completely positive and contractive, so all its values on each fixed test have a bound independent of $n$ and $k$.

<a id="abs:multiplied-quotient-norm"></a>
The relevant quotient norm errors are multiplied errors. If $d\in C_A$ and $a\in A$, then $$\label{abs:multiplied-quotient-norm}
 \|da\|,\ \|ad\|\le\|q(d)\|\,\|a\|.$$ Indeed, replacing $d$ by $d+j$ with $j\in\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A)$ does not change either product, and taking the infimum over such $j$ gives the inequalities. Apply this to $$d=L_n(xy)-L_n(x)L_n(y),\qquad d=L_n(1)-e,$$ where $e$ represents the unit as above. Equations <a href="#abs:quotient-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:quotient-approximation">[abs:quotient-approximation]</a> and <a href="#abs:multiplied-quotient-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:multiplied-quotient-norm">[abs:multiplied-quotient-norm]</a> give errors tending to zero in $A_\omega$ after multiplication by each fixed $a\in A$. Also $[L_n(x),a]=0$ exactly for every $n,x,a$.

Choose a countable dense set $\{a_j\}$ in $A$, and diagonalize over $\mathcal Z_0$, its product tests, and these $a_j$. More explicitly, choose increasing row indices $m_l$ so that the first $l$ multiplied defects in row $m_l$ have ultrapower norm less than $2^{-l-1}$. Their coordinate errors, together with the first $l$ commutator errors, are less than $2^{-l}$ on some set $E_l\in\omega$; replacing these sets by their successive finite intersections with $\{k\ge l\}$ makes them decreasing. Let $l(k)$ be the largest $l\le k$ with $k\in E_l$, or zero when no such level exists. Put $\gamma_k=L_{m_{l(k)},k}$ if $l(k)>0$, and $\gamma_k=0$ otherwise. Then $l(k)\to_\omega\infty$, and $$\Gamma(x)=[(\gamma_k(x))_k]$$ defines a c.p.c. map $\Gamma:\mathcal Z\to A_\omega\cap A'$. The maps $\gamma_k$ are linear and completely positive at every coordinate; no separate lifting of individual elements is involved. The diagonal conditions say $$(\Gamma(xy)-\Gamma(x)\Gamma(y))a=0,\qquad
 (\Gamma(1)-e)a=0
 \quad(x,y\in\mathcal Z_0,\ a\in A).$$ All the displayed defects are central relative to $A$, so they annihilate it on both sides. Passing through $q$ therefore gives multiplicativity and unitality on $\mathcal Z_0$. Continuity extends these identities to $\mathcal Z$ and proves the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
#### Connected half-flip unitaries and their lifts

<a id="stmt-abs-connected-unitaries"></a>
<div id="abs:connected-unitaries" class="lemma">

**Lemma 47**. *The unitary groups of $\mathcal Z$ and $\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z$ are path connected. If $q:D\to D/J$ is a unital quotient map, every unitary in the identity component of $\mathcal U(D/J)$ lifts to a unitary in $D$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-abs-connected-unitaries"><summary>Proof of stmt-abs-connected-unitaries</summary>

*Proof.* We first check connectedness for a prime block $I(r,s)$, with $\gcd(r,s)=1$. A unitary is a continuous path in $U(rs)$ whose endpoints belong to $$H_0=U(r)\otimes1_s,\qquad H_1=1_r\otimes U(s).$$ Both endpoint groups are connected. Attach a path in $H_0$ from $1$ to the initial value and a path in $H_1$ from the final value to $1$. Attaching these paths gradually, while reparametrizing the original path, is a homotopy through paths satisfying the same endpoint conditions. The result is a based loop in $U(rs)$. Changing the attached $H_0$ path by an endpoint loop changes its determinant winding by an arbitrary multiple of $s$; changing the $H_1$ path changes it by an arbitrary multiple of $r$. Bezout’s identity therefore permits choices for which the total winding is zero. The determinant identifies $\pi_1(U(rs))$ with $\mathbb Z$, so this loop contracts with its basepoint fixed. That contraction retains endpoint value $1$, and hence remains in the prime block. Thus every unitary of $I(r,s)$ is connected to $1$.

For a unitary in the injective unital inductive limit $\mathcal Z$, choose a sufficiently close element of a stage. Its two unitary defects are small, so its polar part is a unitary in the same stage and is still close to the original unitary. Two unitaries of distance less than $1$ are joined by the logarithm path for their quotient. Connectedness of the stage unitary group proves connectedness of $\mathcal U(\mathcal Z)$. Strong self-absorption gives an isomorphism $\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z\cong\mathcal Z$, proving the same assertion for the tensor square.

Finally, a unitary $v$ connected to $1$ in $D/J$ is a finite product of exponentials of selfadjoints. To see this, subdivide a continuous unitary path finely enough that consecutive values differ by less than $1$, and take a selfadjoint logarithm of each consecutive quotient. Lift these finitely many selfadjoints through $q$ and multiply their exponentials in $D$. The resulting unitary lifts $v$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion"></a>
<div id="abs:finite-unitary-criterion" class="lemma">

<a id="abs:unitary-criterion"></a>
**Lemma 48**. *Let $A$ be a separable $C^*$-algebra, and suppose there is a unital homomorphism $\varphi:\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. For finite sets $F\subset A$, $G\subset A\otimes\mathcal Z$, and $\varepsilon>0$, there is a unitary $u\in A^{\sim}\otimes\mathcal Z$ such that $$\label{abs:unitary-criterion}
 \|[u,a\otimes1]\|<\varepsilon\quad(a\in F),\qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(u^*bu,A\otimes1)<\varepsilon\quad(b\in G).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion"><summary>Proof of stmt-abs-finite-unitary-criterion</summary>

*Proof.* Write $D=A^{\sim}\otimes\mathcal Z$ and $A_1=A\otimes1$, and form the unital algebra and its annihilator quotient $$C_D=D_\omega\cap A_1',\qquad
 Q=C_D/\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}_{D_\omega}(A_1).$$ First-factor sequences give a homomorphism $F_\omega(A)\to Q$: centrality and annihilation are both preserved by tensoring with $1$. This homomorphism is unital, since the approximate-unit representative $e\otimes1$ differs from $1_{D_\omega}$ by an annihilator of $A_1$. Its composition with $\varphi$ is a unital map $\varphi_1:\mathcal Z\to Q$ and commutes with the constant second-factor map $\varphi_2(z)=1\otimes z+\mathop{\mathrm{Ann}}(A_1)$. Because $\mathcal Z$ is nuclear, these two maps give a unital homomorphism $$\Theta:\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z\longrightarrow Q,
 \qquad \Theta(z\otimes z')=\varphi_1(z)\varphi_2(z').$$

<a id="abs:half-flip"></a>
The approximately inner half-flip of the strongly self-absorbing algebra $\mathcal Z$ <a id="pm-cite-67"></a>[[TW2007](#ref-TW2007), Proposition 1.5] says that, for a finite set $H\subset\mathcal Z$ and $\delta>0$, there is a unitary $V\in\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z$ with $$\label{abs:half-flip}
 \|V^*(1\otimes z)V-z\otimes1\|<\delta\quad(z\in H).$$ By Lemma <a href="#abs:connected-unitaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:connected-unitaries">47</a>, $V$ is connected to $1$. Thus $\Theta(V)$ is connected to $1$ in $Q$, and the same lemma lifts it to a unitary $W\in C_D$.

<a id="abs:lift-centrality"></a>
This unitary has a representing sequence of actual unitaries in $D$. Indeed choose bounded representatives $x_k$. Since $W$ is unitary, both $\|x_k^*x_k-1\|$ and $\|x_kx_k^*-1\|$ tend to zero along $\omega$. On a good set both are less than $1/2$, and the polar parts $u_k=x_k(x_k^*x_k)^{-1/2}$ are unitaries with $\|u_k-x_k\|\to_\omega0$. Put $u_k=1$ on the remaining coordinates. Then $W=[(u_k)]$, and $W\in C_D$ gives $$\label{abs:lift-centrality}
 \|[u_k,a\otimes1]\|\longrightarrow_\omega0\quad(a\in A).$$

<a id="abs:half-flip-multiplied"></a>
For each $z\in H$ choose a lift $c_z\in A_\omega\cap A'$ of $\varphi(z)$; a single c.p.c. lift of $\varphi$ is available by Choi–Effros lifting, so these lifts may satisfy $\|c_z\|\le\|z\|$. In $C_D$ the difference $$d_z=W^*(1\otimes z)W-c_z\otimes1$$ has quotient norm less than $\delta$ by <a href="#abs:half-flip" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:half-flip">[abs:half-flip]</a>. The analogue of <a href="#abs:multiplied-quotient-norm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:multiplied-quotient-norm">[abs:multiplied-quotient-norm]</a> gives $$\|(a\otimes1)d_z\|\le\delta\|a\|.$$ Since $W$ commutes with $a\otimes1$ in the ultrapower, this is the concrete norm estimate $$\label{abs:half-flip-multiplied}
 \|W^*(a\otimes z)W-(ac_z)\otimes1\|\le\delta\|a\|.$$ Here $(ac_z)\otimes1$ is represented by a first-factor sequence $(ac_{z,k})\otimes1$, with $ac_{z,k}\in A$. Thus no estimate in an annihilator quotient is being used as an unmultiplied estimate in a norm ultrapower.

For finitely many elementary tensors, choose one coordinate where <a href="#abs:lift-centrality" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:lift-centrality">[abs:lift-centrality]</a> and <a href="#abs:half-flip-multiplied" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:half-flip-multiplied">[abs:half-flip-multiplied]</a> all have the desired accuracy. This gives <a href="#abs:unitary-criterion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:unitary-criterion">[abs:unitary-criterion]</a> for those tensors. For a general finite $G$, approximate each of its elements by a finite sum of elementary tensors, include all the corresponding second factors in $H$, and choose $\delta$ small enough for the finite sums of the bounds $\delta\|a\|$. Unitary conjugation preserves the initial approximation errors, so the same conclusion holds for $G$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### The final intertwining

The construction of the central map used nuclearity and condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>. The implication from that map to absorption requires only separability, as the following proposition makes explicit.

<a id="stmt-prop-absorption"></a>
<div id="prop:absorption" class="proposition">

**Proposition 49**. *If a separable $C^*$-algebra $A$ admits a unital homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$, then $A\cong A\otimes\mathcal Z$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-absorption"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-absorption</summary>

*Proof.* The zero algebra is immediate, so assume $A\ne0$. Set $B=A\otimes\mathcal Z$ and let $\iota(a)=a\otimes1$. The algebra $A^{\sim}\otimes\mathcal Z$ acts by multipliers on its ideal $B$, so all the unitaries in Lemma <a href="#abs:finite-unitary-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:finite-unitary-criterion">48</a> act as automorphisms of $B$ by conjugation.

<a id="abs:successive-embeddings"></a>
Choose sequences $(a_j)$ and $(b_j)$ dense in $A$ and $B$, respectively, and put $\varepsilon_n=2^{-n}$. We construct products of multiplier unitaries $U_n$ and embeddings $\iota_n=\mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}(U_n)\circ\iota$ as follows. Start with $U_0=1$. Before step $n$, let $F_n$ be a finite subset of $A$ containing $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ and all elements $c_{k,j}$ chosen at earlier steps $k<n$, $1\le j\le k$. Apply Lemma <a href="#abs:finite-unitary-criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="abs:finite-unitary-criterion">48</a> with these domain tests and the finite target set $$\{U_{n-1}^*b_jU_{n-1}:1\le j\le n\}.$$ Obtain $u_n$ with errors less than $\varepsilon_n$, and put $U_n=U_{n-1}u_n$. The commutator estimate gives $$\label{abs:successive-embeddings}
 \|\iota_n(a)-\iota_{n-1}(a)\|<\varepsilon_n
 \quad(a\in F_n),$$ and the range estimate gives elements $c_{n,j}\in A$ with <a id="abs:stored-preimages"></a>$$\label{abs:stored-preimages}
 \|\iota_n(c_{n,j})-b_j\|<\varepsilon_n
 \quad(1\le j\le n).$$ Store these finitely many preimages in every subsequent $F_m$. They are legitimate finite norm tests: each has finite norm, indeed $\|c_{n,j}\|<\|b_j\|+\varepsilon_n$, because $\iota_n$ is isometric.

For each dense domain element $a_j$, equation <a href="#abs:successive-embeddings" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="abs:successive-embeddings">[abs:successive-embeddings]</a> holds at every step $n\ge j$. Summability of $(\varepsilon_n)$ gives convergence of $\iota_n(a_j)$. As all $\iota_n$ are isometric, approximation by the dense set extends this to point-norm convergence on $A$. The limit $\Phi:A\to B$ is a homomorphism by continuity of multiplication and adjoints, and it is isometric since $\|\Phi(a)\|=\lim_n\|\iota_n(a)\|=\|a\|$.

Fix $j$ and take any $n\ge j$. The stored preimage $c_{n,j}$ belongs to every $F_m$ with $m>n$, so $$\|\Phi(c_{n,j})-b_j\|
 \le\varepsilon_n+\sum_{m>n}\varepsilon_m=2^{1-n}.$$ Letting $n\to\infty$ shows that every $b_j$ is in the closure of $\Phi(A)$. The range of an isometric homomorphism is closed, and the $b_j$ are dense, so $\Phi(A)=B$. This proves the desired isomorphism. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:assembly"></a>
### Assembly of the absorption theorems

The preceding construction has used separability, nuclearity, and condition (C), rather than the canonical-map hypothesis. We now apply it to the comparison and Cuntz-semigroup criteria in the introduction.

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-absorption-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-absorption</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a>.* Fix the free ultrafilter in the theorem. Proposition <a href="#prop:Z-map" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:Z-map">46</a>, through the support, norm-cone, tracial-cone, comparison, and packing constructions above, supplies a unital $*$-homomorphism $\mathcal Z\to F_\omega(A)$. Proposition <a href="#prop:absorption" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:absorption">49</a> then gives $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-absorption-3"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-absorption</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#ext:thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:thm:main">1</a>.* The zero algebra, if admitted by the convention for simplicity, is immediate. Nuclearity implies exactness, so Corollary <a href="#ext:cor:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:cor:condition-C">12</a> shows that $A$ satisfies condition <span class="upright">(C)</span>. Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a> then gives $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-absorption-4"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-absorption</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:canonical" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:canonical">3</a>.* Again the zero algebra is immediate. Lemma <a href="#lem:C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:C">15</a> derives (C) from the canonical induced map $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota)$, using that $\mathrm{Cu}(\iota)([b])=[b\otimes1_\mathcal Z]$. Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a> then proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-absorption-5"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-absorption</summary>

*Proof of Corollary <a href="#cor:abstract-cu" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:abstract-cu">4</a>.* The forward implication follows from functoriality of $\mathrm{Cu}$ under $*$-isomorphisms. For the converse, let $\Phi:\mathrm{Cu}(A)\to\mathrm{Cu}(A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z)$ be an abstract isomorphism. The case $A=0$ is immediate, so put $D=A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$ and suppose $A\ne0$. Then $D$ is nonzero, separable, and nuclear.

Strong self-absorption of $\mathcal Z$ supplies an isomorphism $\varphi:\mathcal Z\to\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z$ approximately unitarily equivalent to $z\mapsto z\otimes1_\mathcal Z$ <a id="pm-cite-68"></a>[[TW2007](#ref-TW2007), Definition 1.3(iv) and Example 1.14(iv)]. Consequently $\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_A\otimes\varphi:D\to D\otimes\mathcal Z$ is an isomorphism approximately unitarily equivalent to the first-factor map $j_D(d)=d\otimes1_\mathcal Z$. This also holds when $A$ is nonunital: if $u_n\in\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z$ are the implementing unitaries, then $1_{M(A)}\otimes u_n\in M(A\otimes\mathcal Z\otimes\mathcal Z)$ implement the equivalence, first on elementary tensors and then on all of $D$ by density.

Approximately unitarily equivalent homomorphisms induce the same map on $\mathrm{Cu}$. Indeed, after stabilization, norm approximation by unitary conjugates gives comparison of every positive cut of either image with the other image, by Lemma <a href="#lem:pre:cut-placement" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pre:cut-placement">5</a>(1); approximate units place the comparison witnesses in the algebra when the conjugating unitaries are multipliers. Taking the suprema of cuts in Equation <a href="#eq:pre:cuts" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pre:cuts">[eq:pre:cuts]</a> gives equality of the two Cuntz classes. Thus $\mathrm{Cu}(j_D)=\mathrm{Cu}(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_A\otimes\varphi)$ is an isomorphism. Lemma <a href="#lem:C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:C">15</a>, applied to $D$, gives condition <span class="upright">(C)</span> on $\mathrm{Cu}(D)$. Both almost unperforation and the full almost-divisibility sandwich are intrinsic ordered-semigroup properties, so $\Phi$ transports them to $\mathrm{Cu}(A)$. Theorem <a href="#thm:condition-C" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:condition-C">2</a> now gives $A\cong A\otimes_{\min}\mathcal Z$. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "rounding and comparison arithmetic $↓$ $↓$ transfer of tensor regularity Under separability and nuclearity: $↓$ bounded comparison witnesses and central slots $↓$ central defect filler and dimension-drop maps $𝒵 → F_{ω}(A) ⇒ A ≅ A ⊗ 𝒵$ The order of the constructions. The simple comparison route supplies condition (C) already under exactness. Both absorption routes then use the same separable nuclear construction. The norm-cone multiplicities are fixed before the localized trace accuracies.",
      "figure_id": "fig:proof-flow",
      "pdf_page": 5,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:proof-flow",
          "exact_quote": "The order of the constructions. The simple comparison route\nsupplies condition (C) already under exactness. Both absorption routes\nthen use the same separable nuclear construction. The norm-cone\nmultiplicities are fixed before the localized",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "Flow diagram depicting the order of constructions. At the top level, two boxed nodes:\n- Left box: \"Simple exact non-elementary algebra: extended-functional comparison\" with a downward arrow labeled \"rounding and comparison arithmetic $\\downarrow$\".\n- Right box: \"Separable nuclear algebra: abstract $\\mathrm{Cu}(A) \\cong \\mathrm{Cu}(A \\otimes \\mathcal{Z})$\" with a downward arrow labeled \"$\\downarrow$ transfer of tensor regularity\".\nBoth arrows point into a central box:\n- \"Condition (C): almost unperforation and full almost divisibility\".\nBelow this box is the header text \"Under separability and nuclearity:\" followed by two side-by-side boxes:\n- Left box: \"Norm-visible cones fix the coordinate multiplicities\".\n- Right box: \"Localized tracial cones choose relative errors afterwards\".\nBeneath these two boxes are two successive downward flow steps:\n- \"$\\downarrow$ bounded comparison witnesses and central slots\"\n- \"$\\downarrow$ central defect filler and dimension-drop maps\"\nLeading to the final displayed line:\n- \"$\\mathcal{Z} \\longrightarrow F_\\omega(A) \\implies A \\cong A \\otimes \\mathcal{Z}$\"."
    }
  ],
